                                "type": "text",
                                "text": "     1\t---\n     2\tname: pdf\n     3\tdescription: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n     4\tlicense: Proprietary. LICENSE.txt has complete terms\n     5\t---\n     6\t\n     7\t# PDF Processing Guide\n     8\t\n     9\t## Overview\n    10\t\n    11\tThis guide covers essential PDF processing operations using Python libraries and command-line tools. For advanced features, JavaScript libraries, and detailed examples, see REFERENCE.md. If you need to fill out a PDF form, read FORMS.md and follow its instructions.\n    12\t\n    13\t## Quick Start\n    14\t\n    15\t```python\n    16\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter\n    17\t\n    18\t# Read a PDF\n    19\treader = PdfReader(\"document.pdf\")\n    20\tprint(f\"Pages: {len(reader.pages)}\")\n    21\t\n    22\t# Extract text\n    23\ttext = \"\"\n    24\tfor page in reader.pages:\n    25\t    text += page.extract_text()\n    26\t```\n    27\t\n    28\t## Python Libraries\n    29\t\n    30\t### pypdf - Basic Operations\n    31\t\n    32\t#### Merge PDFs\n    33\t```python\n    34\tfrom pypdf import PdfWriter, PdfReader\n    35\t\n    36\twriter = PdfWriter()\n    37\tfor pdf_file in [\"doc1.pdf\", \"doc2.pdf\", \"doc3.pdf\"]:\n    38\t    reader = PdfReader(pdf_file)\n    39\t    for page in reader.pages:\n    40\t        writer.add_page(page)\n    41\t\n    42\twith open(\"merged.pdf\", \"wb\") as output:\n    43\t    writer.write(output)\n    44\t```\n    45\t\n    46\t#### Split PDF\n    47\t```python\n    48\treader = PdfReader(\"input.pdf\")\n    49\tfor i, page in enumerate(reader.pages):\n    50\t    writer = PdfWriter()\n    51\t    writer.add_page(page)\n    52\t    with open(f\"page_{i+1}.pdf\", \"wb\") as output:\n    53\t        writer.write(output)\n    54\t```\n    55\t\n    56\t#### Extract Metadata\n    57\t```python\n    58\treader = PdfReader(\"document.pdf\")\n    59\tmeta = reader.metadata\n    60\tprint(f\"Title: {meta.title}\")\n    61\tprint(f\"Author: {meta.author}\")\n    62\tprint(f\"Subject: {meta.subject}\")\n    63\tprint(f\"Creator: {meta.creator}\")\n    64\t```\n    65\t\n    66\t#### Rotate Pages\n    67\t```python\n    68\treader = PdfReader(\"input.pdf\")\n    69\twriter = PdfWriter()\n    70\t\n    71\tpage = reader.pages[0]\n    72\tpage.rotate(90)  # Rotate 90 degrees clockwise\n    73\twriter.add_page(page)\n    74\t\n    75\twith open(\"rotated.pdf\", \"wb\") as output:\n    76\t    writer.write(output)\n    77\t```\n    78\t\n    79\t### pdfplumber - Text and Table Extraction\n    80\t\n    81\t#### Extract Text with Layout\n    82\t```python\n    83\timport pdfplumber\n    84\t\n    85\twith pdfplumber.open(\"document.pdf\") as pdf:\n    86\t    for page in pdf.pages:\n    87\t        text = page.extract_text()\n    88\t        print(text)\n    89\t```\n    90\t\n    91\t#### Extract Tables\n    92\t```python\n    93\twith pdfplumber.open(\"document.pdf\") as pdf:\n    94\t    for i, page in enumerate(pdf.pages):\n    95\t        tables = page.extract_tables()\n    96\t        for j, table in enumerate(tables):\n    97\t            print(f\"Table {j+1} on page {i+1}:\")\n    98\t            for row in table:\n    99\t                print(row)\n   100\t```\n   101\t\n   102\t#### Advanced Table Extraction\n   103\t```python\n   104\timport pandas as pd\n   105\t\n   106\twith pdfplumber.open(\"document.pdf\") as pdf:\n   107\t    all_tables = []\n   108\t    for page in pdf.pages:\n   109\t        tables = page.extract_tables()\n   110\t        for table in tables:\n   111\t            if table:  # Check if table is not empty\n   112\t                df = pd.DataFrame(table[1:], columns=table[0])\n   113\t                all_tables.append(df)\n   114\t\n   115\t# Combine all tables\n   116\tif all_tables:\n   117\t    combined_df = pd.concat(all_tables, ignore_index=True)\n   118\t    combined_df.to_excel(\"extracted_tables.xlsx\", index=False)\n   119\t```\n   120\t\n   121\t### reportlab - Create PDFs\n   122\t\n   123\t#### Basic PDF Creation\n   124\t```python\n   125\tfrom reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter\n   126\tfrom reportlab.pdfgen import canvas\n   127\t\n   128\tc = canvas.Canvas(\"hello.pdf\", pagesize=letter)\n   129\twidth, height = letter\n   130\t\n   131\t# Add text\n   132\tc.drawString(100, height - 100, \"Hello World!\")\n   133\tc.drawString(100, height - 120, \"This is a PDF created with reportlab\")\n   134\t\n   135\t# Add a line\n   136\tc.line(100, height - 140, 400, height - 140)\n   137\t\n   138\t# Save\n   139\tc.save()\n   140\t```\n   141\t\n   142\t#### Create PDF with Multiple Pages\n   143\t```python\n   144\tfrom reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter\n   145\tfrom reportlab.platypus import SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, PageBreak\n   146\tfrom reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet\n   147\t\n   148\tdoc = SimpleDocTemplate(\"report.pdf\", pagesize=letter)\n   149\tstyles = getSampleStyleSheet()\n   150\tstory = []\n   151\t\n   152\t# Add content\n   153\ttitle = Paragraph(\"Report Title\", styles['Title'])\n   154\tstory.append(title)\n   155\tstory.append(Spacer(1, 12))\n   156\t\n   157\tbody = Paragraph(\"This is the body of the report. \" * 20, styles['Normal'])\n   158\tstory.append(body)\n   159\tstory.append(PageBreak())\n   160\t\n   161\t# Page 2\n   162\tstory.append(Paragraph(\"Page 2\", styles['Heading1']))\n   163\tstory.append(Paragraph(\"Content for page 2\", styles['Normal']))\n   164\t\n   165\t# Build PDF\n   166\tdoc.build(story)\n   167\t```\n   168\t\n   169\t#### Subscripts and Superscripts\n   170\t\n   171\t**IMPORTANT**: Never use Unicode subscript/superscript characters (\u2080\u2081\u2082\u2083\u2084\u2085\u2086\u2087\u2088\u2089, \u2070\u00b9\u00b2\u00b3\u2074\u2075\u2076\u2077\u2078\u2079) in ReportLab PDFs. The built-in fonts do not include these glyphs, causing them to render as solid black boxes.\n   172\t\n   173\tInstead, use ReportLab's XML markup tags in Paragraph objects:\n   174\t```python\n   175\tfrom reportlab.platypus import Paragraph\n   176\tfrom reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet\n   177\t\n   178\tstyles = getSampleStyleSheet()\n   179\t\n   180\t# Subscripts: use <sub> tag\n   181\tchemical = Paragraph(\"H<sub>2</sub>O\", styles['Normal'])\n   182\t\n   183\t# Superscripts: use <super> tag\n   184\tsquared = Paragraph(\"x<super>2</super> + y<super>2</super>\", styles['Normal'])\n   185\t```\n   186\t\n   187\tFor canvas-drawn text (not Paragraph objects), manually adjust font the size and position rather than using Unicode subscripts/superscripts.\n   188\t\n   189\t## Command-Line Tools\n   190\t\n   191\t### pdftotext (poppler-utils)\n   192\t```bash\n   193\t# Extract text\n   194\tpdftotext input.pdf output.txt\n   195\t\n   196\t# Extract text preserving layout\n   197\tpdftotext -layout input.pdf output.txt\n   198\t\n   199\t# Extract specific pages\n   200\tpdftotext -f 1 -l 5 input.pdf output.txt  # Pages 1-5\n   201\t```\n   202\t\n   203\t### qpdf\n   204\t```bash\n   205\t# Merge PDFs\n   206\tqpdf --empty --pages file1.pdf file2.pdf -- merged.pdf\n   207\t\n   208\t# Split pages\n   209\tqpdf input.pdf --pages . 1-5 -- pages1-5.pdf\n   210\tqpdf input.pdf --pages . 6-10 -- pages6-10.pdf\n   211\t\n   212\t# Rotate pages\n   213\tqpdf input.pdf output.pdf --rotate=+90:1  # Rotate page 1 by 90 degrees\n   214\t\n   215\t# Remove password\n   216\tqpdf --password=mypassword --decrypt encrypted.pdf decrypted.pdf\n   217\t```\n   218\t\n   219\t### pdftk (if available)\n   220\t```bash\n   221\t# Merge\n   222\tpdftk file1.pdf file2.pdf cat output merged.pdf\n   223\t\n   224\t# Split\n   225\tpdftk input.pdf burst\n   226\t\n   227\t# Rotate\n   228\tpdftk input.pdf rotate 1east output rotated.pdf\n   229\t```\n   230\t\n   231\t## Common Tasks\n   232\t\n   233\t### Extract Text from Scanned PDFs\n   234\t```python\n   235\t# Requires: pip install pytesseract pdf2image\n   236\timport pytesseract\n   237\tfrom pdf2image import convert_from_path\n   238\t\n   239\t# Convert PDF to images\n   240\timages = convert_from_path('scanned.pdf')\n   241\t\n   242\t# OCR each page\n   243\ttext = \"\"\n   244\tfor i, image in enumerate(images):\n   245\t    text += f\"Page {i+1}:\\n\"\n   246\t    text += pytesseract.image_to_string(image)\n   247\t    text += \"\\n\\n\"\n   248\t\n   249\tprint(text)\n   250\t```\n   251\t\n   252\t### Add Watermark\n   253\t```python\n   254\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter\n   255\t\n   256\t# Create watermark (or load existing)\n   257\twatermark = PdfReader(\"watermark.pdf\").pages[0]\n   258\t\n   259\t# Apply to all pages\n   260\treader = PdfReader(\"document.pdf\")\n   261\twriter = PdfWriter()\n   262\t\n   263\tfor page in reader.pages:\n   264\t    page.merge_page(watermark)\n   265\t    writer.add_page(page)\n   266\t\n   267\twith open(\"watermarked.pdf\", \"wb\") as output:\n   268\t    writer.write(output)\n   269\t```\n   270\t\n   271\t### Extract Images\n   272\t```bash\n   273\t# Using pdfimages (poppler-utils)\n   274\tpdfimages -j input.pdf output_prefix\n   275\t\n   276\t# This extracts all images as output_prefix-000.jpg, output_prefix-001.jpg, etc.\n   277\t```\n   278\t\n   279\t### Password Protection\n   280\t```python\n   281\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter\n   282\t\n   283\treader = PdfReader(\"input.pdf\")\n   284\twriter = PdfWriter()\n   285\t\n   286\tfor page in reader.pages:\n   287\t    writer.add_page(page)\n   288\t\n   289\t# Add password\n   290\twriter.encrypt(\"userpassword\", \"ownerpassword\")\n   291\t\n   292\twith open(\"encrypted.pdf\", \"wb\") as output:\n   293\t    writer.write(output)\n   294\t```\n   295\t\n   296\t## Quick Reference\n   297\t\n   298\t| Task | Best Tool | Command/Code |\n   299\t|------|-----------|--------------|\n   300\t| Merge PDFs | pypdf | `writer.add_page(page)` |\n   301\t| Split PDFs | pypdf | One page per file |\n   302\t| Extract text | pdfplumber | `page.extract_text()` |\n   303\t| Extract tables | pdfplumber | `page.extract_tables()` |\n   304\t| Create PDFs | reportlab | Canvas or Platypus |\n   305\t| Command line merge | qpdf | `qpdf --empty --pages ...` |\n   306\t| OCR scanned PDFs | pytesseract | Convert to image first |\n   307\t| Fill PDF forms | pdf-lib or pypdf (see FORMS.md) | See FORMS.md |\n   308\t\n   309\t## Next Steps\n   310\t\n   311\t- For advanced pypdfium2 usage, see REFERENCE.md\n   312\t- For JavaScript libraries (pdf-lib), see REFERENCE.md\n   313\t- If you need to fill out a PDF form, follow the instructions in FORMS.md\n   314\t- For troubleshooting guides, see REFERENCE.md\n   315\t",
                                "uuid": "2d84766d-b067-41c0-b409-1fcd82e74730"
                            }

}\n  .cover-meta b { color: #2b1f22; }\n\n  h2.section {\n    font-size: 15pt;\n    color: #9A2A3F;\n    border-bottom: 2px solid #9A2A3F;\n    padding-bottom: 6px;\n    margin-top: 8px;\n    margin-bottom: 14px;\n    font-weight: 700;\n  }\n  h3.sub {\n    font-size: 11.5pt;\n    color: #2b1f22;\n    margin-top: 20px;\n    margin-bottom: 8px;\n    font-weight: 700;\n  }\n  p { margin: 0 0 10px 0; }\n  ul, ol { margin: 6px 0 14px 0; padding-left: 20px; }\n  li { margin-bottom: 5px; }\n\n  .callout {\n    background: #FBF3EE;\n    border-left: 4px solid #9A2A3F;\n    padding: 12px 16px;\n    margin: 14px 0 20px 0;\n    font-size: 10pt;\n    color: #4a3a3d;\n  }\n\n  table {\n    width: 100%;\n    border-collapse: collapse;\n    margin-bottom: 18px;\n    font-size: 9.8pt;\n  }\n  th {\n    background: #2b1f22;\n    color: #fff;\n    text-align: left;\n    padding: 8px 10px;\n    font-weight: 600;\n  }\n  td {\n    padding: 7px 10px;\n    border-bottom: 1px solid #e8e0e1;\n  }\n  tr:nth-child(even) td { background: #FBF3EE; }\n  td.num, th.num { text-align: right; white-space: nowrap; }\n  tr.total td {\n    border-top: 2px solid #2b1f22;\n    font-weight: 700;\n    background: #f4e9ea;\n  }\n\n  .phase-header {\n    background: #2b1f22;\n    color: #fff;\n    padding: 14px 18px;\n    border-radius: 4px;\n    margin: 22px 0 14px 0;\n  }\n  .phase-header .ptitle { font-size: 13pt; font-weight: 700; margin-bottom: 3px; }\n  .phase-header .pmeta { font-size: 9.5pt; color: #d9c9cb; }\n  .phase-header .pprice { float: right; font-size: 16pt; font-weight: 700; color: #f0d9dc; }\n\n  .kpi-grid {\n    display: table;\n    width: 100%;\n    margin-bottom: 18px;\n    border-spacing: 8px 0;\n  }\n  .kpi-row { display: table-row; }\n  .kpi-cell {\n    display: table-cell;\n    width: 25%;\n    background: #FBF3EE;\n    border: 1px solid #eaddDF;\n    text-align: center;\n    padding: 12px 6px;\n    border-radius: 4px;\n  }\n  .kpi-num { font-size: 15pt; font-weight: 700; color: #9A2A3F; display: block; }\n  .kpi-label { font-size: 8pt; color: #6b5b5e; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.5px; }\n\n  .two-col { display: table; width: 100%; margin-bottom: 16px; }\n  .two-col .col { display: table-cell; width: 50%; vertical-align: top; padding-right: 14px; }\n  .two-col .col:last-child { padding-right: 0; padding-left: 14px; }\n\n  .footnote { font-size: 8.5pt; color: #8a7a7d; margin-top: 4px; }\n\n  .signature-block {\n    display: table;\n    width: 100%;\n    margin-top: 40px;\n  }\n  .sig-col { display: table-cell; width: 50%; }\n  .sig-line {\n    border-top: 1px solid #999;\n    margin-top: 40px;\n    padding-top: 6px;\n    font-size: 9pt;\n    color: #555;\n  }\n\n  .page-break { page-break-before: always; }\n\n  .closing-box {\n    background: #2b1f22;\n    color: #f5ece d;\n    color: #f2e8e9;\n    padding: 20px 22px;\n    border-radius: 6px;\n    margin-top: 10px;\n  }\n  .closing-box h3 { color: #fff; margin-top: 0; }\n  .closing-box p { color: #e6d9db; }\n\n  .validity {\n    font-size: 9pt;\n    color: #8a7a7d;\n    text-align: center;\n    margin-top: 30px;\n    border-top: 1px solid #e8e0e1;\n    padding-top: 10px;\n  }\n</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n\n<!-- COVER PAGE -->\n<div class=\"cover\">\n  <div class=\"tag\">Marketing Proposal &amp; Quotation</div>\n  <h1>Taking Your Chunari Sarees<br>Online</h1>\n  <div class=\"cover-rule\"></div>\n  <div class=\"sub\">A phased social-commerce launch plan built for a first-time online venture</div>\n\n  <div class=\"cover-meta\">\n    <div><b>Prepared for:</b> [Client / Business Name]</div>\n    <div><b>Prepared by:</b> Vraj Vithalani</div>\n    <div><b>Date:</b> 11 August 2026</div>\n    <div><b>Validity:</b> This quotation is valid for 15 days from the date above</div>\n  </div>\n</div>\n\n<!-- PAGE 1: INTRO -->\n<h2 class=\"section\">Understanding Your Business</h2>\n<p>\nYour family has built a strong wholesale presence in chunari sarees over 15 years, with an established\ndistribution footprint across Gujarat, Madhya Pradesh, and Uttar Pradesh. This proposal is for the next\nchapter \u2014 taking that same craftsmanship and trust directly to individual customers, pan-India, through\nsocial media.\n</p>\n<p>\nSince this is a first-time online launch \u2014 no existing page, no content, no prior digital sales \u2014 the\nright approach is to prove the model works before scaling spend on it. This proposal is structured in\ntwo phases for exactly that reason.\n</p>\n\n<div class=\"callout\">\n<b>Our approach in one line:</b> Start lean, get real orders flowing through Instagram and WhatsApp,\nand only scale the budget once results are visible \u2014 with Phase 2 timed to land right at the festive\nseason (Navratri &amp; Diwali), when demand for bandhani and festive sarees peaks.\n</div>\n\n<h3 class=\"sub\">Where We'll Sell</h3>\n<div class=\"two-col\">\n  <div class=\"col\">\n    <p><b>Instagram \u2014 for discovery.</b> Reels showing the drape, fall, and colour of each saree are the\n    most effective way to get a brand-new page seen by the right audience, at the lowest cost.</p>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"col\">\n    <p><b>WhatsApp \u2014 for closing the sale.</b> Most customers in India prefer to finalise a purchase over\n    WhatsApp. We'll set up a proper Catalog with clear pricing, so browsing turns into orders smoothly.</p>\n  </div>\n</div>\n<p class=\"footnote\">\nA website is intentionally not part of Phase 1 \u2014 it isn't needed to start selling, and keeping the\nbudget focused on Instagram + WhatsApp gets you to real orders faster. We recommend revisiting a simple\nwebsite once the online leg is established, mainly for consistent business information across the web.\n</p>\n\n<div class=\"page-break\"></div>\n\n<!-- PHASE 1 -->\n<h2 class=\"section\">Phase 1 \u2014 Proof of Concept</h2>\n<p>The goal of Phase 1 is simple: launch professionally, generate genuine first orders, and prove the\nmodel with minimal risk \u2014 before any bigger investment is made.</p>\n\n<div class=\"phase-header\">\n  <span class=\"pprice\">\u20b912,000<span style=\"font-size:9pt; font-weight:400;\"> /month</span></span>\n  <div class=\"ptitle\">Phase 1: Launch &amp; Proof</div>\n  <div class=\"pmeta\">Duration: 2 months &nbsp;\u2022&nbsp; Total investment: \u20b924,000</div>\n</div>\n\n<table>\n  <tr><th>Investment Area</th><th class=\"num\">Monthly</th></tr>\n  <tr><td>Instagram &amp; WhatsApp advertising (audience reach + orders)</td><td class=\"num\">\u20b96,000</td></tr>\n  <tr><td>Content creation &amp; day-to-day execution</td><td class=\"num\">\u20b94,000</td></tr>\n  <tr><td>Tools, design software &amp; catalog setup</td><td class=\"num\">\u20b91,000</td></tr>\n  <tr><td>Strategy, planning &amp; account management</td><td class=\"num\">\u20b91,000</td></tr>\n  <tr class=\"total\"><td>Total</td><td class=\"num\">\u20b912,000 / month</td></tr>\n</table>\n\n<h3 class=\"sub\">What's Included</h3>\n<div class=\"two-col\">\n  <div class=\"col\">\n    <ul>\n      <li>Instagram &amp; Facebook page setup and optimisation</li>\n      <li>WhatsApp Business Catalog setup with clear pricing</li>\n      <li>Google Business Profile creation</li>\n      <li>3 Reels + 2\u20133 posts per week</li>\n      <li>Daily Stories &amp; WhatsApp Status</li>\n    </ul>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"col\">\n    <ul>\n      <li>Targeted ad campaigns, optimised weekly</li>\n      <li>Order enquiry &amp; WhatsApp response handling</li>\n      <li>Order-confirmation process to reduce returns</li>\n      <li>Weekly progress updates on WhatsApp</li>\n      <li>Full results report at the end of Phase 1</li>\n    </ul>\n  </div>\n</div>\n\n<h3 class=\"sub\">What You Can Realistically Expect (8 weeks)</h3>\n<div class=\"kpi-grid\">\n  <div class=\"kpi-row\">\n    <div class=\"kpi-cell\"><span class=\"kpi-num\">1,000\u20133,000</span><span class=\"kpi-label\">New Followers</span></div>\n    <div class=\"kpi-cell\"><span class=\"kpi-num\">120\u2013150</span><span class=\"kpi-label\">Buyer Conversations</span></div>\n    <div class=\"kpi-cell\"><span class=\"kpi-num\">12\u201322</span><span class=\"kpi-label\">Orders</span></div>\n    <div class=\"kpi-cell\"><span class=\"kpi-num\">\u20b98k\u201314k</span><span class=\"kpi-label\">Revenue Generated</span></div>\n  </div>\n</div>\n<p class=\"footnote\">\nThese are realistic, research-backed estimates for a brand-new page with this budget \u2014 not a guarantee.\nThe first two weeks focus on setup; enquiries typically pick up from week 3\u20134 onward as ads optimise.\n</p>\n\n<div class=\"page-break\"></div>\n\n<!-- PHASE 2 -->\n<h2 class=\"section\">Phase 2 \u2014 Scale-Up</h2>\n<p>Once Phase 1 proves the model works, Phase 2 builds on it \u2014 with a bigger budget, deeper content,\nand timing aligned to the festive season, when demand for chunari and bandhani sarees is at its highest.</p>\n\n<div class=\"phase-header\">\n  <span class=\"pprice\">\u20b925,000\u201330,000<span style=\"font-size:9pt; font-weight:400;\"> /month</span></span>\n  <div class=\"ptitle\">Phase 2: Scale &amp; Festive Push</div>\n  <div class=\"pmeta\">Starts: Month 3 onward &nbsp;\u2022&nbsp; Timed for Navratri &amp; Diwali 2026</div>\n</div>\n\n<table>\n  <tr><th>Investment Area</th><th class=\"num\">Monthly Range</th></tr>\n  <tr><td>Instagram &amp; WhatsApp advertising (scaled)</td><td class=\"num\">\u20b915,000 \u2013 17,000</td></tr>\n  <tr><td>Expanded content &amp; execution</td><td class=\"num\">\u20b96,000 \u2013 8,000</td></tr>\n  <tr><td>Strategy, planning &amp; account management</td><td class=\"num\">\u20b95,000 \u2013 7,000</td></tr>\n  <tr><td>Tools &amp; nano-influencer collaborations</td><td class=\"num\">\u20b93,000 \u2013 5,000</td></tr>\n  <tr class=\"total\"><td>Total</td><td class=\"num\">\u20b925,000 \u2013 30,000 / month</td></tr>\n</table>\n\n<h3 class=\"sub\">What Changes in Phase 2</h3>\n<ul>\n  <li><b>Bigger ad budget, better efficiency</b> \u2014 at this spend level, advertising platforms optimise\n  properly, which typically lowers the cost of each order compared to Phase 1.</li>\n  <li><b>More content, more often</b> \u2014 expanded Reels output, customer testimonials, and real\n  order-packing content that builds trust.</li>\n  <li><b>Nano-influencer collaborations</b> \u2014 3\u20135 small, relevant creators to extend reach beyond organic\n  followers.</li>\n  <li><b>Festive campaign calendar</b> \u2014 campaigns built specifically around Navratri and Diwali, when\n  demand for festive sarees is highest.</li>\n  <li><b>Deeper reporting</b> \u2014 fortnightly performance dashboards instead of a single end-of-phase report.</li>\n</ul>\n\n<div class=\"callout\">\n<b>Why the increase is worth it:</b> Phase 1 exists to prove the model at low risk. Phase 2 spends more\nbecause it now has data, a warm audience, and proof behind it \u2014 which means every rupee works harder,\nright as festive demand peaks.\n</div>\n\n<h3 class=\"sub\">Also Available From Phase 2 Onward</h3>\n<p>A simple website (for consistent business information online), Instagram Shop, and \u2014 as volume grows \u2014\nlisting on wholesale-friendly marketplaces for additional reach.</p>\n\n<div class=\"page-break\"></div>\n\n<h2 class=\"section\">Why This Structure</h2>\n<div class=\"two-col\">\n  <div class=\"col\">\n    <h3 class=\"sub\">Low risk, real proof first</h3>\n    <p>Rather than asking for a large budget upfront on an unproven channel, Phase 1 is deliberately\n    lean \u2014 enough to generate genuine orders and prove the model works, before any bigger commitment.</p>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"col\">\n    <h3 class=\"sub\">Timed to your advantage</h3>\n    <p>Phase 1 runs through the setup months; Phase 2 scales right into Navratri and Diwali 2026 \u2014\n    exactly when festive and bandhani saree demand is at its annual peak.</p>\n  </div>\n</div>\n\n<h3 class=\"sub\">Payment Terms</h3>\n<ul>\n  <li>Phase 1: 100% payable at the start of each month</li>\n  <li>Ad spend is billed separately and directly through the ad platform, at cost \u2014 no markup</li>\n  <li>Phase 2 begins only once Phase 1 results are shared and reviewed together</li>\n</ul>\n\n<div class=\"closing-box\">\n  <h3>Ready to Begin</h3>\n  <p>This proposal is designed to get your online journey started the right way \u2014 carefully, with real\n  proof at every step. We're looking forward to building this with you.</p>\n</div>\n\n<div class=\"signature-block\">\n  <div class=\"sig-col\">\n    <div class=\"sig-line\">For [Client / Business Name] \u2014 Signature &amp; Date</div>\n  </div>\n  <div class=\"sig-col\">\n    <div class=\"sig-line\">Vraj Vithalani \u2014 Signature &amp; Date</div>\n  </div>\n</div>\n\n<div class=\"validity\">\nThis quotation is valid for 15 days from the date of issue &nbsp;|&nbsp; Prepared by Vraj Vithalani &nbsp;|&nbsp; 11 August 2026\n</div>\n\n</body>\n</html>\n"
                        },

This proposal is structured in\\ntwo phases for exactly that reason.\\n</p>\\n\\n<div class=\\\"callout\\\">\\n<b>Our approach in one line:</b> Start lean, get real orders flowing through Instagram and WhatsApp,\\nand only scale the budget once results are visible \\u2014 with Phase 2 timed to land right at the festive\\nseason (Navratri &amp; Diwali), when demand for bandhani and festive sarees peaks.\\n</div>\\n\\n<h3 class=\\\"sub\\\">Where We'll Sell</h3>\\n<div class=\\\"two-col\\\">\\n  <div class=\\\"col\\\">\\n    <p><b>Instagram \\u2014 for discovery.</b> Reels showing the drape, fall, and colour of each saree are the\\n    most effective way to get a brand-new page seen by the right audience, at the lowest cost.</p>\\n  </div>\\n  <div class=\\\"col\\\">\\n    <p><b>WhatsApp \\u2014 for closing the sale.</b> Most customers in India prefer to finalise a purchase over\\n    WhatsApp. We'll set up a proper Catalog with clear pricing, so browsing turns into orders smoothly.</p>\\n  </div>\\n</div>\\n<p class=\\\"footnote\\\">\\nA website is intentionally not part of Phase 1 \\u2014 it isn't needed to start selling, and keeping the\\nbudget focused on Instagram + WhatsApp gets you to real orders faster. We recommend revisiting a simple\\nwebsite once the online leg is established, mainly for consistent business information across the web.\\n</p>\\n\\n<div class=\\\"page-break\\\"></div>\\n\\n<!-- PHASE 1 -->\\n<h2 class=\\\"section\\\">Phase 1 \\u2014 Proof of Concept</h2>\\n<p>The goal of Phase 1 is simple: launch professionally, generate genuine first orders, and prove the\\nmodel with minimal risk \\u2014 before any bigger investment is made.</p>\\n\\n<div class=\\\"phase-header\\\">\\n  <span class=\\\"pprice\\\">\\u20b912,000<span style=\\\"font-size:9pt; font-weight:400;\\\"> /month</span></span>\\n  <div class=\\\"ptitle\\\">Phase 1: Launch &amp; Proof</div>\\n  <div class=\\\"pmeta\\\">Duration: 2 months &nbsp;\\u2022&nbsp; Total investment: \\u20b924,000</div>\\n</div>\\n\\n<table>\\n  <tr><th>Investment Area</th><th class=\\\"num\\\">Monthly</th></tr>\\n  <tr><td>Instagram &amp; WhatsApp advertising (audience reach + orders)</td><td class=\\\"num\\\">\\u20b96,000</td></tr>\\n  <tr><td>Content creation &amp; day-to-day execution</td><td class=\\\"num\\\">\\u20b94,000</td></tr>\\n  <tr><td>Tools, design software &amp; catalog setup</td><td class=\\\"num\\\">\\u20b91,000</td></tr>\\n  <tr><td>Strategy, planning &amp; account management</td><td class=\\\"num\\\">\\u20b91,000</td></tr>\\n  <tr class=\\\"total\\\"><td>Total</td><td class=\\\"num\\\">\\u20b912,000 / month</td></tr>\\n</table>\\n\\n<h3 class=\\\"sub\\\">What's Included</h3>\\n<div class=\\\"two-col\\\">\\n  <div class=\\\"col\\\">\\n    <ul>\\n      <li>Instagram &amp; Facebook page setup and optimisation</li>\\n      <li>WhatsApp Business Catalog setup with clear pricing</li>\\n      <li>Google Business Profile creation</li>\\n      <li>3 Reels + 2\\u20133 posts per week</li>\\n      <li>Daily Stories &amp; WhatsApp Status</li>\\n    </ul>\\n  </div>\\n  <div class=\\\"col\\\">\\n    <ul>\\n      <li>Targeted ad campaigns, optimised weekly</li>\\n      <li>Order enquiry &amp; WhatsApp response handling</li>\\n      <li>Order-confirmation process to reduce returns</li>\\n      <li>Weekly progress updates on WhatsApp</li>\\n      <li>Full results report at the end of Phase 1</li>\\n    </ul>\\n  </div>\\n</div>\\n\\n<h3 class=\\\"sub\\\">What You Can Realistically Expect (8 weeks)</h3>\\n<div class=\\\"kpi-grid\\\">\\n  <div class=\\\"kpi-row\\\">\\n    <div class=\\\"kpi-cell\\\"><span class=\\\"kpi-num\\\">1,000\\u20133,000</span><span class=\\\"kpi-label\\\">New Followers</span></div>\\n    <div class=\\\"kpi-cell\\\"><span class=\\\"kpi-num\\\">120\\u2013150</span><span class=\\\"kpi-label\\\">Buyer Conversations</span></div>\\n    <div class=\\\"kpi-cell\\\"><span class=\\\"kpi-num\\\">12\\u201322</span><span class=\\\"kpi-label\\\">Orders</span></div>\\n    <div class=\\\"kpi-cell\\\"><span class=\\\"kpi-num\\\">\\u20b98k\\u201314k</span><span class=\\\"kpi-label\\\">Revenue Generated</span></div>\\n  </div>\\n</div>\\n<p class=\\\"footnote\\\">\\nThese are realistic, research-backed estimates for a brand-new page with this budget \\u2014 not a guarantee.\\nThe first two weeks focus on setup; enquiries typically pick up from week 3\\u20134 onward as ads optimise.\\n</p>\\n\\n<div class=\\\"page-break\\\"></div>\\n\\n<!-- PHASE 2 -->\\n<h2 class=\\\"section\\\">Phase 2 \\u2014 Scale-Up</h2>\\n<p>Once Phase 1 proves the model works, Phase 2 builds on it \\u2014 with a bigger budget, deeper content,\\nand timing aligned to the festive season, when demand for chunari and bandhani sarees is at its highest.</p>\\n\\n<div class=\\\"phase-header\\\">\\n  <span class=\\\"pprice\\\">\\u20b925,000\\u201330,000<span style=\\\"font-size:9pt; font-weight:400;\\\"> /month</span></span>\\n  <div class=\\\"ptitle\\\">Phase 2: Scale &amp; Festive Push</div>\\n  <div class=\\\"pmeta\\\">Starts: Month 3 onward &nbsp;\\u2022&nbsp; Timed for Navratri &amp; Diwali 2026</div>\\n</div>\\n\\n<table>\\n  <tr><th>Investment Area</th><th class=\\\"num\\\">Monthly Range</th></tr>\\n  <tr><td>Instagram &amp; WhatsApp advertising (scaled)</td><td class=\\\"num\\\">\\u20b915,000 \\u2013 17,000</td></tr>\\n  <tr><td>Expanded content &amp; execution</td><td class=\\\"num\\\">\\u20b96,000 \\u2013 8,000</td></tr>\\n  <tr><td>Strategy, planning &amp; account management</td><td class=\\\"num\\\">\\u20b95,000 \\u2013 7,000</td></tr>\\n  <tr><td>Tools &amp; nano-influencer collaborations</td><td class=\\\"num\\\">\\u20b93,000 \\u2013 5,000</td></tr>\\n  <tr class=\\\"total\\\"><td>Total</td><td class=\\\"num\\\">\\u20b925,000 \\u2013 30,000 / month</td></tr>\\n</table>\\n\\n<h3 class=\\\"sub\\\">What Changes in Phase 2</h3>\\n<ul>\\n  <li><b>Bigger ad budget, better efficiency</b> \\u2014 at this spend level, advertising platforms optimise\\n  properly, which typically lowers the cost of each order compared to Phase 1.</li>\\n  <li><b>More content, more often</b> \\u2014 expanded Reels output, customer testimonials, and real\\n  order-packing content that builds trust.</li>\\n  <li><b>Nano-influencer collaborations</b> \\u2014 3\\u20135 small, relevant creators to extend reach beyond organic\\n  followers.</li>\\n  <li><b>Festive campaign calendar</b> \\u2014 campaigns built specifically around Navratri and Diwali, when\\n  demand for festive sarees is highest.</li>\\n  <li><b>Deeper reporting</b> \\u2014 fortnightly performance dashboards instead of a single end-of-phase report.</li>\\n</ul>\\n\\n<div class=\\\"callout\\\">\\n<b>Why the increase is worth it:</b> Phase 1 exists to prove the model at low risk. Phase 2 spends more\\nbecause it now has data, a warm audience, and proof behind it \\u2014 which means every rupee works harder,\\nright as festive demand peaks.\\n</div>\\n\\n<h3 class=\\\"sub\\\">Also Available From Phase 2 Onward</h3>\\n<p>A simple website (for consistent business information online), Instagram Shop, and \\u2014 as volume grows \\u2014\\nlisting on wholesale-friendly marketplaces for additional reach.</p>\\n\\n<div class=\\\"page-break\\\"></div>\\n\\n<h2 class=\\\"section\\\">Why This Structure</h2>\\n<div class=\\\"two-col\\\">\\n  <div class=\\\"col\\\">\\n    <h3 class=\\\"sub\\\">Low risk, real proof first</h3>\\n    <p>Rather than asking for a large budget upfront on an unproven channel, Phase 1 is deliberately\\n    lean \\u2014 enough to generate genuine orders and prove the model works, before any bigger commitment.</p>\\n  </div>\\n  <div class=\\\"col\\\">\\n    <h3 class=\\\"sub\\\">Timed to your advantage</h3>\\n    <p>Phase 1 runs through the setup months; Phase 2 scales right into Navratri and Diwali 2026 \\u2014\\n    exactly when festive and bandhani saree demand is at its annual peak.</p>\\n  </div>\\n</div>\\n\\n<h3 class=\\\"sub\\\">Payment Terms</h3>\\n<ul>\\n  <li>Phase 1: 100% payable at the start of each month</li>\\n  <li>Ad spend is billed separately and directly through the ad platform, at cost \\u2014 no markup</li>\\n  <li>Phase 2 begins only once Phase 1 results are shared and reviewed together</li>\\n</ul>\\n\\n<div class=\\\"closing-box\\\">\\n  <h3>Ready to Begin</h3>\\n  <p>This proposal is designed to get your online journey started the right way \\u2014 carefully, with real\\n  proof at every step. We're looking forward to building this with you.</p>\\n</div>\\n\\n<div class=\\\"signature-block\\\">\\n  <div class=\\\"sig-col\\\">\\n    <div class=\\\"sig-line\\\">For [Client / Business Name] \\u2014 Signature &amp; Date</div>\\n  </div>\\n  <div class=\\\"sig-col\\\">\\n    <div class=\\\"sig-line\\\">Vraj Vithalani \\u2014 Signature &amp; Date</div>\\n  </div>\\n</div>\\n\\n<div class=\\\"validity\\\">\\nThis quotation is valid for 15 days from the date of issue &nbsp;|&nbsp; Prepared by Vraj Vithalani &nbsp;|&nbsp; 11 August 2026\\n</div>\\n\\n</body>\\n</html>\\n\", \"filename\": \"/home/claude/proposal/proposal.html\"}"

}\n  ul, ol { margin: 6px 0 16px 0; padding-left: 20px; }\n  li { margin-bottom: 8px; }\n\n  .callout {\n    background: #FBF3EE;\n    border-left: 4px solid #9A2A3F;\n    padding: 12px 14px;\n    margin: 14px 0 20px 0;\n    font-size: 11.5pt;\n    color: #4a3a3d;\n  }\n\n  /* Tables become stacked cards on narrow width instead of side-by-side columns */\n  table {\n    width: 100%;\n    border-collapse: collapse;\n    margin-bottom: 18px;\n    font-size: 11pt;\n  }\n  th {\n    background: #2b1f22;\n    color: #fff;\n    text-align: left;\n    padding: 8px 8px;\n    font-weight: 600;\n  }\n  td {\n    padding: 9px 8px;\n    border-bottom: 1px solid #e8e0e1;\n  }\n  tr:nth-child(even) td { background: #FBF3EE; }\n  td.num, th.num { text-align: right; white-space: nowrap; }\n  tr.total td {\n    border-top: 2px solid #2b1f22;\n    font-weight: 700;\n    background: #f4e9ea;\n  }\n\n  .phase-header {\n    background: #2b1f22;\n    color: #fff;\n    padding: 14px 16px;\n    border-radius: 6px;\n    margin: 20px 0 14px 0;\n  }\n  .phase-header .ptitle { font-size: 13.5pt; font-weight: 700; margin-bottom: 6px; }\n  .phase-header .pmeta { font-size: 9.5pt; color: #d9c9cb; line-height: 1.5; }\n  .phase-header .pprice { font-size: 17pt; font-weight: 700; color: #f0d9dc; display: block; margin-bottom: 4px; }\n\n  /* KPI grid: 2 columns instead of 4, so numbers stay readable on a narrow page */\n  .kpi-grid {\n    display: table;\n    width: 100%;\n    margin-bottom: 18px;\n    border-spacing: 8px 8px;\n  }\n  .kpi-row { display: table-row; }\n  .kpi-cell {\n    display: table-cell;\n    width: 50%;\n    background: #FBF3EE;\n    border: 1px solid #eaddDF;\n    text-align: center;\n    padding: 14px 8px;\n    border-radius: 6px;\n  }\n  .kpi-num { font-size: 16pt; font-weight: 700; color: #9A2A3F; display: block; margin-bottom: 3px; }\n  .kpi-label { font-size: 8.5pt; color: #6b5b5e; text-transform: uppercase; letter-spacing: 0.5px; }\n\n  /* Two-column layout becomes single column stack on narrow page */\n  .two-col { width: 100%; margin-bottom: 16px; }\n  .two-col .col { width: 100%; margin-bottom: 16px; }\n  .two-col .col:last-child { margin-bottom: 0; }\n\n  .footnote { font-size: 9.5pt; color: #8a7a7d; margin-top: 4px; line-height: 1.5; }\n\n  .signature-block { width: 100%; margin-top: 30px; }\n  .sig-col { width: 100%; margin-bottom: 28px; }\n  .sig-line {\n    border-top: 1px solid #999;\n    margin-top: 34px;\n    padding-top: 6px;\n    font-size: 10pt;\n    color: #555;\n  }\n\n  .page-break { page-break-before: always; }\n\n  .closing-box {\n    background: #2b1f22;\n    color: #f2e8e9;\n    padding: 18px 16px;\n    border-radius: 8px;\n    margin-top: 10px;\n  }\n  .closing-box h3 { color: #fff; margin-top: 0; font-size: 13pt; }\n  .closing-box p { color: #e6d9db; margin-bottom: 0; }\n\n  .validity {\n    font-size: 9pt;\n    color: #8a7a7d;\n    text-align: center;\n    margin-top: 24px;\n    border-top: 1px solid #e8e0e1;\n    padding-top: 10px;\n    line-height: 1.6;\n  }"
                        },

                        "flags": null,
                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "Confirmed \u2014 the 2\u00d72 KPI grid is splitting mid-block across a page break, which looks broken. I'll add a `page-break-inside: avoid` on the grid and a couple other blocks (phase-header, table, callout) so they don't tear across pages.",

                            "old_str": "  .kpi-grid {\n    display: table;\n    width: 100%;\n    margin-bottom: 18px;\n    border-spacing: 8px 8px;\n  }",

                    {
                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-11T13:06:21.317049Z",
                        "stop_timestamp": "2026-08-11T13:06:23.365916Z",
                        "flags": null,
                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "This version is sized like a phone screen (portrait, narrow, large text) so it opens readable in WhatsApp/PDF viewers without pinch-zooming \u2014 12 pages, but each one is short and scannable.\n\nTwo things before you send it:\n- **\"[Client / Business Name]\"** is still a placeholder on the cover and signature line \u2014 tell me the actual name and I'll swap it in.\n- I kept the original A4 desktop version too, in case you also email/print it somewhere formal.",
                        "citations": []
                    }
                ],

            }

    {
        "uuid": "82dba497-143c-44db-a98d-328bd5700a08",
        "name": "Trust audit reports and 12A/80G certification",
        "summary": "",
        "created_at": "2026-08-13T05:23:06.559444Z",
        "updated_at": "2026-08-13T05:23:31.264357Z",
        "account": {
            "uuid": "271dba8a-47e6-483f-9a1d-d504ad12c578"
        },
        "chat_messages": [
            {
                "uuid": "019ff992-f9f6-7871-bc77-fc91c4e2e7b4",
                "text": "Hey as you know I'm dealing with a trust as for now. So I want to know about one thing. As I said the trust was established in 2019 and from then on the trust was not on operation. I somehow managed to start the trust again after the verification and kyc's and all. And now I want to apply for 12A/80G certifications, but for this we need audit reports and we've not filled any from 2019. Now there were no transactions as well, so we're supposed to fill NIL report only. But what are the incometax charges and can I file it myself or I need a CA for it?",
                "content": [
                    {
                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-13T05:23:07.960060Z",
                        "stop_timestamp": "2026-08-13T05:23:07.960060Z",
                        "flags": null,
                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "Hey as you know I'm dealing with a trust as for now. So I want to know about one thing. As I said the trust was established in 2019 and from then on the trust was not on operation. I somehow managed to start the trust again after the verification and kyc's and all. And now I want to apply for 12A/80G certifications, but for this we need audit reports and we've not filled any from 2019. Now there were no transactions as well, so we're supposed to fill NIL report only. But what are the incometax charges and can I file it myself or I need a CA for it?",
                        "citations": []
                    }
                ],
                "sender": "human",
                "created_at": "2026-08-13T05:23:07.960265Z",
                "updated_at": "2026-08-13T05:23:07.960265Z",
                "attachments": [],
                "files": [],
                "parent_message_uuid": "00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000000"
            },
            {
                "uuid": "019ff992-f9f6-7888-83ef-c751aa7a0543",
                "text": "\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\n\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\n\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nThis project's files don't cover this specific tax question \u2014 they're just registration/affidavit documents for your trust. Let me answer from general knowledge instead.\n\nHere's the situation, broken into the two things you're asking:\n\n**Can you self-file, or do you need a CA?**\n\nFor a trust, income tax return filing (ITR-7) and the accompanying audit report (Form 10B/10BB) are two different things:\n\n- **The ITR-7 return itself** \u2014 you technically *can* file this yourself if you're comfortable with the income tax portal, since it doesn't legally require a CA.\n- **The audit report (Form 10B or 10BB)** \u2014 this **must** be filed by a Chartered Accountant. It requires a CA's digital signature and UDIN (Unique Document Identification Number) generated through ICAI. There's no way around this even for a NIL report \u2014 the audit itself is a statutory requirement once a trust crosses the applicability threshold or is applying for 12A/80G, and only a CA can certify it.\n\nSo practically: you'll need a CA at least for the audit report, even if the underlying work is minimal since there were no transactions.\n\n**Backlog filing (2019 to now) and charges**\n\n- Since there's a gap of several years, each year's ITR-7 and Form 10B/10BB (where applicable) generally has to be filed separately for each assessment year, not combined.\n- **Late filing fees**: Section 234F penalty (\u20b91,000\u2013\u20b95,000 depending on the year and whether income exceeded the basic exemption) applies per year if filed after the due date \u2014 though for a NIL-income trust this is often on the lower end or sometimes waived depending on circumstances.\n- **Condonation of delay**: If you're filing return/audit reports for past years beyond the normal time limit, you likely need to apply for **condonation of delay under Section 119(2)(b)** (or the specific circular for delayed Form 10B/10BB, e.g., CBDT circulars periodically extend these) \u2014 this needs to be done before the backlog filings will even be accepted by the portal in some cases.\n- **CA professional fees** (not income tax charges, but their service fee) for NIL audit reports are usually modest since there's no transaction complexity \u2014 but exact fees vary a lot by CA and region, so that's a \"ask a couple of local CAs\" question, not something I can quote precisely.\n- There's no separate \"audit fee\" charged by the Income Tax Department \u2014 the cost you're likely thinking of is the CA's professional fee plus any late filing penalty under 234F.\n\n**Bottom line**: You'll need a CA to sign off on the audit reports (even NIL ones) for each pending year, and likely a condonation-of-delay application before those old years can be filed. The return filing itself you could do yourself, but most people bundle it with the same CA since the audit and return need to reconcile.\n\nSince this involves real penalty calculations and condonation procedure specifics that change with CBDT circulars, it'd be worth a quick search for the current circular before you proceed \u2014 want me to check the latest CBDT guidance on condonation for delayed Form 10B filing?",
                "content": [
                    {

                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "This project's files don't cover this specific tax question \u2014 they're just registration/affidavit documents for your trust. Let me answer from general knowledge instead.\n\nHere's the situation, broken into the two things you're asking:\n\n**Can you self-file, or do you need a CA?**\n\nFor a trust, income tax return filing (ITR-7) and the accompanying audit report (Form 10B/10BB) are two different things:\n\n- **The ITR-7 return itself** \u2014 you technically *can* file this yourself if you're comfortable with the income tax portal, since it doesn't legally require a CA.\n- **The audit report (Form 10B or 10BB)** \u2014 this **must** be filed by a Chartered Accountant. It requires a CA's digital signature and UDIN (Unique Document Identification Number) generated through ICAI. There's no way around this even for a NIL report \u2014 the audit itself is a statutory requirement once a trust crosses the applicability threshold or is applying for 12A/80G, and only a CA can certify it.\n\nSo practically: you'll need a CA at least for the audit report, even if the underlying work is minimal since there were no transactions.\n\n**Backlog filing (2019 to now) and charges**\n\n- Since there's a gap of several years, each year's ITR-7 and Form 10B/10BB (where applicable) generally has to be filed separately for each assessment year, not combined.\n- **Late filing fees**: Section 234F penalty (\u20b91,000\u2013\u20b95,000 depending on the year and whether income exceeded the basic exemption) applies per year if filed after the due date \u2014 though for a NIL-income trust this is often on the lower end or sometimes waived depending on circumstances.\n- **Condonation of delay**: If you're filing return/audit reports for past years beyond the normal time limit, you likely need to apply for **condonation of delay under Section 119(2)(b)** (or the specific circular for delayed Form 10B/10BB, e.g., CBDT circulars periodically extend these) \u2014 this needs to be done before the backlog filings will even be accepted by the portal in some cases.\n- **CA professional fees** (not income tax charges, but their service fee) for NIL audit reports are usually modest since there's no transaction complexity \u2014 but exact fees vary a lot by CA and region, so that's a \"ask a couple of local CAs\" question, not something I can quote precisely.\n- There's no separate \"audit fee\" charged by the Income Tax Department \u2014 the cost you're likely thinking of is the CA's professional fee plus any late filing penalty under 234F.\n\n**Bottom line**: You'll need a CA to sign off on the audit reports (even NIL ones) for each pending year, and likely a condonation-of-delay application before those old years can be filed. The return filing itself you could do yourself, but most people bundle it with the same CA since the audit and return need to reconcile.\n\nSince this involves real penalty calculations and condonation procedure specifics that change with CBDT circulars, it'd be worth a quick search for the current circular before you proceed \u2014 want me to check the latest CBDT guidance on condonation for delayed Form 10B filing?",
                        "citations": []
                    }
                ],

                "uuid": "019ffb4e-cb6e-7ae2-a9b2-648c1705ac16",
                "text": "Here's a realistic cost breakdown for premium WooCommerce hosting for a Pan-India luxury store:\n\n## Hosting Options (Best-tier for WooCommerce)\n\n**Managed WooCommerce Hosting** (recommended for luxury/high-conversion stores)\n| Provider | Plan | Monthly Cost (\u20b9) |\n|---|---|---|\n| Cloudways (DigitalOcean/Vultr HF) | Business tier | 4,000\u20138,000 |\n| Kinsta (WooCommerce plans) | Business 1\u20132 | 8,000\u201315,000 |\n| WP Engine (WooCommerce) | Growth/Scale | 10,000\u201320,000 |\n| Nestify (India-based) | Business | 3,500\u20137,000 |\n| SiteGround | GoGeek | 3,000\u20135,000 |\n\n**Enterprise-grade** (if you expect high traffic/scale)\n- AWS/GCP with managed WooCommerce setup (via a DevOps partner): \u20b915,000\u201340,000+/month depending on server specs, CDN, and traffic\n- Cloudways + dedicated server: \u20b910,000\u201325,000/month\n\n## Additional Costs to Budget For\n\n- **Domain**: \u20b9800\u20131,500/year\n- **SSL**: Usually free (Let's Encrypt) or \u20b95,000\u201315,000/year for premium/EV SSL (recommended for luxury brand trust)\n- **CDN** (Cloudflare Pro/Business or BunnyCDN): \u20b91,500\u20138,000/month\n- **Backup & security** (Sucuri, Wordfence Premium): \u20b91,500\u20135,000/month\n- **Premium WooCommerce extensions** (payment gateways, inventory, shipping for Pan-India logistics): \u20b95,000\u201320,000/year\n- **Premium theme/page builder**: \u20b93,000\u201315,000 one-time or annual\n\n## Key Considerations for a Pan-India Luxury Store\n\n- **Server location**: Choose a data center in Mumbai (AWS ap-south-1, DigitalOcean Bangalore) for lower latency across India rather than Singapore/US\n- **CDN is essential**: Pan-India delivery needs fast image/asset loading nationwide \u2014 luxury customers expect zero lag\n- **Payment gateway compatibility**: Ensure hosting supports Razorpay/PayU/CCAvenue smoothly with SSL\n- **Traffic scaling**: Luxury stores often see spiky traffic (sales, launches) \u2014 pick a plan with easy scaling (Cloudways/Kinsta handle this well)\n\n## Realistic Total Monthly Budget\n\n- **Starting out**: \u20b98,000\u201315,000/month (hosting + CDN + security)\n- **Established/scaling**: \u20b925,000\u201350,000/month\n\nWant me to help you compare 2-3 specific providers in more detail based on your expected traffic and product catalog size?",

                        "text": "Here's a realistic cost breakdown for premium WooCommerce hosting for a Pan-India luxury store:\n\n## Hosting Options (Best-tier for WooCommerce)\n\n**Managed WooCommerce Hosting** (recommended for luxury/high-conversion stores)\n| Provider | Plan | Monthly Cost (\u20b9) |\n|---|---|---|\n| Cloudways (DigitalOcean/Vultr HF) | Business tier | 4,000\u20138,000 |\n| Kinsta (WooCommerce plans) | Business 1\u20132 | 8,000\u201315,000 |\n| WP Engine (WooCommerce) | Growth/Scale | 10,000\u201320,000 |\n| Nestify (India-based) | Business | 3,500\u20137,000 |\n| SiteGround | GoGeek | 3,000\u20135,000 |\n\n**Enterprise-grade** (if you expect high traffic/scale)\n- AWS/GCP with managed WooCommerce setup (via a DevOps partner): \u20b915,000\u201340,000+/month depending on server specs, CDN, and traffic\n- Cloudways + dedicated server: \u20b910,000\u201325,000/month\n\n## Additional Costs to Budget For\n\n- **Domain**: \u20b9800\u20131,500/year\n- **SSL**: Usually free (Let's Encrypt) or \u20b95,000\u201315,000/year for premium/EV SSL (recommended for luxury brand trust)\n- **CDN** (Cloudflare Pro/Business or BunnyCDN): \u20b91,500\u20138,000/month\n- **Backup & security** (Sucuri, Wordfence Premium): \u20b91,500\u20135,000/month\n- **Premium WooCommerce extensions** (payment gateways, inventory, shipping for Pan-India logistics): \u20b95,000\u201320,000/year\n- **Premium theme/page builder**: \u20b93,000\u201315,000 one-time or annual\n\n## Key Considerations for a Pan-India Luxury Store\n\n- **Server location**: Choose a data center in Mumbai (AWS ap-south-1, DigitalOcean Bangalore) for lower latency across India rather than Singapore/US\n- **CDN is essential**: Pan-India delivery needs fast image/asset loading nationwide \u2014 luxury customers expect zero lag\n- **Payment gateway compatibility**: Ensure hosting supports Razorpay/PayU/CCAvenue smoothly with SSL\n- **Traffic scaling**: Luxury stores often see spiky traffic (sales, launches) \u2014 pick a plan with easy scaling (Cloudways/Kinsta handle this well)\n\n## Realistic Total Monthly Budget\n\n- **Starting out**: \u20b98,000\u201315,000/month (hosting + CDN + security)\n- **Established/scaling**: \u20b925,000\u201350,000/month\n\nWant me to help you compare 2-3 specific providers in more detail based on your expected traffic and product catalog size?",

                    }

            {
                "uuid": "019ffb50-1b20-7d79-85b7-00ba851aee21",
                "text": "The store is luxary but not the budget. It's 50k inr only for complete website include development cost, hosting cost, plugin cost, everything.",
                "content": [
                    {
                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-13T13:29:19.438237Z",
                        "stop_timestamp": "2026-08-13T13:29:19.438237Z",
                        "flags": null,
                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "The store is luxary but not the budget. It's 50k inr only for complete website include development cost, hosting cost, plugin cost, everything.",
                        "citations": []
                    }
                ],
                "sender": "human",
                "created_at": "2026-08-13T13:29:19.438485Z",
                "updated_at": "2026-08-13T13:29:19.438485Z",
                "attachments": [],

            {
                "uuid": "019ffb50-1b20-7d80-9682-dcfd266314c3",
                "text": "With \u20b950,000 total (one-time dev + hosting + plugins), here's how that realistically breaks down \u2014 you'll need to be strategic since \u20b950k is tight for a \"luxury-feel\" custom build.\n\n## Realistic Allocation\n\n| Item | Cost (\u20b9) |\n|---|---|\n| Hosting (1 year) | 4,000\u20138,000 |\n| Domain | 800\u20131,200 |\n| Premium theme (luxury-style, e.g. Flatsome, Woodmart) | 3,000\u20135,000 |\n| SSL | Free (Let's Encrypt) |\n| Essential paid plugins (payment gateway, shipping, SEO) | 3,000\u20138,000 |\n| Development (self-build or freelancer) | 25,000\u201335,000 |\n| **Total** | **~40,000\u201350,000** |\n\n## Key Trade-offs at This Budget\n\n**Hosting**: Skip Kinsta/WP Engine \u2014 go with **Hostinger Business/Cloud** or **Cloudways (DO basic)** at \u20b9300\u2013600/month. Perfectly fine for a launch-stage store; you can upgrade later once you have revenue/traffic.\n\n**Development**: This is where \u20b950k gets tight. Your real options:\n- **DIY with a premium theme** (Woodmart, Flatsome, Porto \u2014 \u20b93,000\u20135,000) + drag-and-drop builder (Elementor). This is how most \"luxury-looking\" WooCommerce stores on small budgets are actually built \u2014 the theme does the heavy lifting for visual polish.\n- **Freelancer** (not agency) for setup/customization \u2014 \u20b915,000\u201330,000 for a template-based build. A fully custom design from an agency will blow past \u20b950k easily (agencies typically start at \u20b975k\u20132L+ for custom WooCommerce builds).\n\n**Plugins** \u2014 prioritize only what you truly need:\n- Payment gateway (Razorpay \u2014 free plugin, transaction fees only)\n- WooCommerce Shipping/India-specific shipping plugin (Shiprocket integration \u2014 often free tier available)\n- SEO (Rank Math free version is excellent, skip premium initially)\n- Skip heavy premium plugin bundles at launch\n\n## Honest Advice\n\n\"Luxury feel\" at \u20b950k comes from **design discipline, not budget** \u2014 good typography, whitespace, high-quality product photography, and a clean premium theme will do more for perceived luxury than expensive plugins or hosting. Spend saved money on **professional product photography** instead if you can \u2014 that matters more visually than backend infrastructure at this stage.\n\nWant help picking a specific theme + plugin stack that fits this budget, or a rough week-by-week plan if you're building it yourself?",
                "content": [
                    {
                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-13T13:29:19.432217Z",
                        "stop_timestamp": "2026-08-13T13:29:30.115204Z",

                        "text": "With \u20b950,000 total (one-time dev + hosting + plugins), here's how that realistically breaks down \u2014 you'll need to be strategic since \u20b950k is tight for a \"luxury-feel\" custom build.\n\n## Realistic Allocation\n\n| Item | Cost (\u20b9) |\n|---|---|\n| Hosting (1 year) | 4,000\u20138,000 |\n| Domain | 800\u20131,200 |\n| Premium theme (luxury-style, e.g. Flatsome, Woodmart) | 3,000\u20135,000 |\n| SSL | Free (Let's Encrypt) |\n| Essential paid plugins (payment gateway, shipping, SEO) | 3,000\u20138,000 |\n| Development (self-build or freelancer) | 25,000\u201335,000 |\n| **Total** | **~40,000\u201350,000** |\n\n## Key Trade-offs at This Budget\n\n**Hosting**: Skip Kinsta/WP Engine \u2014 go with **Hostinger Business/Cloud** or **Cloudways (DO basic)** at \u20b9300\u2013600/month. Perfectly fine for a launch-stage store; you can upgrade later once you have revenue/traffic.\n\n**Development**: This is where \u20b950k gets tight. A fully custom design from an agency will blow past \u20b950k easily (agencies typically start at \u20b975k\u20132L+ for custom WooCommerce builds).\n\n**Plugins** \u2014 prioritize only what you truly need:\n- Payment gateway (Razorpay \u2014 free plugin, transaction fees only)\n- WooCommerce Shipping/India-specific shipping plugin (Shiprocket integration \u2014 often free tier available)\n- SEO (Rank Math free version is excellent, skip premium initially)\n- Skip heavy premium plugin bundles at launch\n\n## Honest Advice\n\n\"Luxury feel\" at \u20b950k comes from **design discipline, not budget** \u2014 good typography, whitespace, high-quality product photography, and a clean premium theme will do more for perceived luxury than expensive plugins or hosting.                 "text": "20k elementor cost, 7k domain and hosting cost, 15k in wordfence security, 18k for Yost. Other then this what would I need to build a woo commerce website and what would that cost? I've got the domain and woo commerce dedicated hosting.",

                        "text": "20k elementor cost, 7k domain and hosting cost, 15k in wordfence security, 18k for Yost. Other then this what would I need to build a woo commerce website and what would that cost? I've got the domain and woo commerce dedicated hosting.",

            {
                "uuid": "019ffb55-d28d-7344-87c9-328a3e3efb18",
                "text": "Quick sanity check first \u2014 those numbers seem way off for what you're describing. Elementor Pro is typically ~$60-200/year, not 20k. Wordfence and Yoast premium are each roughly $100-130/year. Are those figures in a different currency (like INR or PKR), or are they for multi-year/multi-site licenses? That changes the picture a lot.\n\nAssuming these are just your budget line items (whatever currency), here's what else a real WooCommerce store typically needs:\n\n**Essential**\n- **SSL certificate** \u2014 often free/included with hosting, but confirm\n- **Payment gateway** \u2014 Stripe/PayPal/local gateway; usually free to integrate, but they take a % per transaction (2-3% typical)\n- **A WooCommerce-compatible theme** \u2014 either free (Storefront) or premium (~$50-100 one-time)\n- **Product photography/content** \u2014 cost varies wildly depending on if you DIY or hire\n- **Backup plugin** \u2014 UpdraftPlus or similar (~$70/year premium, or free tier)\n- **Transactional email service** \u2014 WP Mail SMTP or similar so order confirmations don't land in spam (~$50-100/year for a paid tier, or free with limits via Mailgun/SendGrid)\n\n**Often needed depending on your store**\n- Shipping plugin/calculator (depends on carrier \u2014 some are free, some paid)\n- Tax calculation plugin (TaxJar, Avalara, or WooCommerce's built-in \u2014 free to paid)\n- Inventory/stock management extras if you have complex SKUs\n- A caching plugin (WP Rocket ~$59/year, or free alternatives) for site speed\n- GDPR/cookie consent plugin (mostly free)\n\n**One-time, not recurring**\n- Developer/agency setup time if you're not building it yourself\n- Logo/branding\n\n**Ongoing beyond what you listed**\n- Plugin/theme renewal fees (annual, not one-time)\n- Site maintenance/updates if you're not doing it yourself\n\nWant me to sketch out a rough total budget once you clarify the currency/period on those first four numbers?",
                "content": [
                    {
                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-13T13:35:34.426258Z",
                        "stop_timestamp": "2026-08-13T13:35:43.344810Z",

            {
                "uuid": "019ffb5e-3909-7e87-9d4a-b2f177ad915d",
                "text": "Got it \u2014 here's the fuller picture, developer-to-developer.\n\n**Email (transactional)**\nDon't rely on Hostinger's default mail for order confirmations \u2014 deliverability is bad. Go with **WP Mail SMTP (free) + Brevo (formerly Sendinblue) free tier** \u2014 300 emails/day free, which covers 5-50 orders/month easily. If it scales, Brevo's paid tier starts around $9-25/month. No need to pay for WP Mail SMTP Pro unless you need advanced routing/logs.\n\n**Tax calculation**\nFor a client at this scale, **use WooCommerce's built-in tax settings** \u2014 don't add an external plugin. Avalara/TaxJar are overkill unless the client sells across multiple states/countries with complex nexus rules (India GST especially \u2014 WooCommerce's native tax classes + manual GST setup handle this fine for most single-country sellers). Only go external if they're crossing into international tax compliance territory later.\n\n**Custom checkout funnel**\nSince you're ditching default WooCommerce flow, you have two paths:\n- **CartFlows** (~$200-300/year for Pro) \u2014 pre-built funnel builder, faster to ship, less flexibility\n- **Fully custom** via Elementor Pro + WooCommerce hooks/custom PHP \u2014 more dev time, but since you already have Elementor Pro, this is likely your better ROI. No extra licensing cost, just your build hours.\n\nGiven you're already deep in Elementor, I'd lean custom-built over CartFlows unless the client wants something turnkey fast.\n\n**Other upfront costs to quote the client**\n\n*Recurring (annual/monthly \u2014 pass through or mark up):*\n- Elementor Pro \u2014 $180/yr\n- Wordfence Premium \u2014 $149/yr\n- Yoast Premium \u2014 $178.80/yr\n- Shiprocket \u2014 ~$2400/yr (starter tier)\n- Hosting/domain \u2014 whatever Hostinger plan\n- Brevo email \u2014 free tier initially\n- Backup plugin \u2014 UpdraftPlus Premium ~$70/yr (don't skip this \u2014 Hostinger backups alone aren't enough for a store with transactional data)\n- SSL \u2014 usually free with Hostinger\n\n*One-time (your dev fee, separate from licenses):*\n- Custom checkout/funnel build\n- Custom shipping calculator logic/integration\n- Theme customization / custom design implementation\n- Product catalog setup & import\n- Payment gateway (Razorpay) integration & testing\n- Staging \u2192 live migration\n- Post-launch QA across devices\n- Client training/handover documentation\n\n*Easy to forget, worth quoting separately:*\n- **Abandoned cart recovery** (built into some email tools, or a plugin like CartFlows/FunnelKit \u2014 helps recoup revenue, good upsell to client)\n- **Order/invoice PDF generation** plugin if they need GST invoices \u2014 free options like PDF Invoices & Packing Slips for WooCommerce\n- **Legal pages** \u2014 privacy policy, refund policy, terms \u2014 templated but need review, especially with GST/e-commerce rules in India\n\nThat should round out your scope of work and pricing sheet.",
                "content": [
                    {
                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-13T13:44:44.978888Z",
                        "stop_timestamp": "2026-08-13T13:44:58.525724Z",
                        "flags": null,

                                "type": "text",
                                "text": "     1\t---\n     2\tname: pdf\n     3\tdescription: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.\n     4\tlicense: Proprietary. LICENSE.txt has complete terms\n     5\t---\n     6\t\n     7\t# PDF Processing Guide\n     8\t\n     9\t## Overview\n    10\t\n    11\tThis guide covers essential PDF processing operations using Python libraries and command-line tools. For advanced features, JavaScript libraries, and detailed examples, see REFERENCE.md. If you need to fill out a PDF form, read FORMS.md and follow its instructions.\n    12\t\n    13\t## Quick Start\n    14\t\n    15\t```python\n    16\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter\n    17\t\n    18\t# Read a PDF\n    19\treader = PdfReader(\"document.pdf\")\n    20\tprint(f\"Pages: {len(reader.pages)}\")\n    21\t\n    22\t# Extract text\n    23\ttext = \"\"\n    24\tfor page in reader.pages:\n    25\t    text += page.extract_text()\n    26\t```\n    27\t\n    28\t## Python Libraries\n    29\t\n    30\t### pypdf - Basic Operations\n    31\t\n    32\t#### Merge PDFs\n    33\t```python\n    34\tfrom pypdf import PdfWriter, PdfReader\n    35\t\n    36\twriter = PdfWriter()\n    37\tfor pdf_file in [\"doc1.pdf\", \"doc2.pdf\", \"doc3.pdf\"]:\n    38\t    reader = PdfReader(pdf_file)\n    39\t    for page in reader.pages:\n    40\t        writer.add_page(page)\n    41\t\n    42\twith open(\"merged.pdf\", \"wb\") as output:\n    43\t    writer.write(output)\n    44\t```\n    45\t\n    46\t#### Split PDF\n    47\t```python\n    48\treader = PdfReader(\"input.pdf\")\n    49\tfor i, page in enumerate(reader.pages):\n    50\t    writer = PdfWriter()\n    51\t    writer.add_page(page)\n    52\t    with open(f\"page_{i+1}.pdf\", \"wb\") as output:\n    53\t        writer.write(output)\n    54\t```\n    55\t\n    56\t#### Extract Metadata\n    57\t```python\n    58\treader = PdfReader(\"document.pdf\")\n    59\tmeta = reader.metadata\n    60\tprint(f\"Title: {meta.title}\")\n    61\tprint(f\"Author: {meta.author}\")\n    62\tprint(f\"Subject: {meta.subject}\")\n    63\tprint(f\"Creator: {meta.creator}\")\n    64\t```\n    65\t\n    66\t#### Rotate Pages\n    67\t```python\n    68\treader = PdfReader(\"input.pdf\")\n    69\twriter = PdfWriter()\n    70\t\n    71\tpage = reader.pages[0]\n    72\tpage.rotate(90)  # Rotate 90 degrees clockwise\n    73\twriter.add_page(page)\n    74\t\n    75\twith open(\"rotated.pdf\", \"wb\") as output:\n    76\t    writer.write(output)\n    77\t```\n    78\t\n    79\t### pdfplumber - Text and Table Extraction\n    80\t\n    81\t#### Extract Text with Layout\n    82\t```python\n    83\timport pdfplumber\n    84\t\n    85\twith pdfplumber.open(\"document.pdf\") as pdf:\n    86\t    for page in pdf.pages:\n    87\t        text = page.extract_text()\n    88\t        print(text)\n    89\t```\n    90\t\n    91\t#### Extract Tables\n    92\t```python\n    93\twith pdfplumber.open(\"document.pdf\") as pdf:\n    94\t    for i, page in enumerate(pdf.pages):\n    95\t        tables = page.extract_tables()\n    96\t        for j, table in enumerate(tables):\n    97\t            print(f\"Table {j+1} on page {i+1}:\")\n    98\t            for row in table:\n    99\t                print(row)\n   100\t```\n   101\t\n   102\t#### Advanced Table Extraction\n   103\t```python\n   104\timport pandas as pd\n   105\t\n   106\twith pdfplumber.open(\"document.pdf\") as pdf:\n   107\t    all_tables = []\n   108\t    for page in pdf.pages:\n   109\t        tables = page.extract_tables()\n   110\t        for table in tables:\n   111\t            if table:  # Check if table is not empty\n   112\t                df = pd.DataFrame(table[1:], columns=table[0])\n   113\t                all_tables.append(df)\n   114\t\n   115\t# Combine all tables\n   116\tif all_tables:\n   117\t    combined_df = pd.concat(all_tables, ignore_index=True)\n   118\t    combined_df.to_excel(\"extracted_tables.xlsx\", index=False)\n   119\t```\n   120\t\n   121\t### reportlab - Create PDFs\n   122\t\n   123\t#### Basic PDF Creation\n   124\t```python\n   125\tfrom reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter\n   126\tfrom reportlab.pdfgen import canvas\n   127\t\n   128\tc = canvas.Canvas(\"hello.pdf\", pagesize=letter)\n   129\twidth, height = letter\n   130\t\n   131\t# Add text\n   132\tc.drawString(100, height - 100, \"Hello World!\")\n   133\tc.drawString(100, height - 120, \"This is a PDF created with reportlab\")\n   134\t\n   135\t# Add a line\n   136\tc.line(100, height - 140, 400, height - 140)\n   137\t\n   138\t# Save\n   139\tc.save()\n   140\t```\n   141\t\n   142\t#### Create PDF with Multiple Pages\n   143\t```python\n   144\tfrom reportlab.lib.pagesizes import letter\n   145\tfrom reportlab.platypus import SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, PageBreak\n   146\tfrom reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet\n   147\t\n   148\tdoc = SimpleDocTemplate(\"report.pdf\", pagesize=letter)\n   149\tstyles = getSampleStyleSheet()\n   150\tstory = []\n   151\t\n   152\t# Add content\n   153\ttitle = Paragraph(\"Report Title\", styles['Title'])\n   154\tstory.append(title)\n   155\tstory.append(Spacer(1, 12))\n   156\t\n   157\tbody = Paragraph(\"This is the body of the report. \" * 20, styles['Normal'])\n   158\tstory.append(body)\n   159\tstory.append(PageBreak())\n   160\t\n   161\t# Page 2\n   162\tstory.append(Paragraph(\"Page 2\", styles['Heading1']))\n   163\tstory.append(Paragraph(\"Content for page 2\", styles['Normal']))\n   164\t\n   165\t# Build PDF\n   166\tdoc.build(story)\n   167\t```\n   168\t\n   169\t#### Subscripts and Superscripts\n   170\t\n   171\t**IMPORTANT**: Never use Unicode subscript/superscript characters (\u2080\u2081\u2082\u2083\u2084\u2085\u2086\u2087\u2088\u2089, \u2070\u00b9\u00b2\u00b3\u2074\u2075\u2076\u2077\u2078\u2079) in ReportLab PDFs. The built-in fonts do not include these glyphs, causing them to render as solid black boxes.\n   172\t\n   173\tInstead, use ReportLab's XML markup tags in Paragraph objects:\n   174\t```python\n   175\tfrom reportlab.platypus import Paragraph\n   176\tfrom reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet\n   177\t\n   178\tstyles = getSampleStyleSheet()\n   179\t\n   180\t# Subscripts: use <sub> tag\n   181\tchemical = Paragraph(\"H<sub>2</sub>O\", styles['Normal'])\n   182\t\n   183\t# Superscripts: use <super> tag\n   184\tsquared = Paragraph(\"x<super>2</super> + y<super>2</super>\", styles['Normal'])\n   185\t```\n   186\t\n   187\tFor canvas-drawn text (not Paragraph objects), manually adjust font the size and position rather than using Unicode subscripts/superscripts.\n   188\t\n   189\t## Command-Line Tools\n   190\t\n   191\t### pdftotext (poppler-utils)\n   192\t```bash\n   193\t# Extract text\n   194\tpdftotext input.pdf output.txt\n   195\t\n   196\t# Extract text preserving layout\n   197\tpdftotext -layout input.pdf output.txt\n   198\t\n   199\t# Extract specific pages\n   200\tpdftotext -f 1 -l 5 input.pdf output.txt  # Pages 1-5\n   201\t```\n   202\t\n   203\t### qpdf\n   204\t```bash\n   205\t# Merge PDFs\n   206\tqpdf --empty --pages file1.pdf file2.pdf -- merged.pdf\n   207\t\n   208\t# Split pages\n   209\tqpdf input.pdf --pages . 1-5 -- pages1-5.pdf\n   210\tqpdf input.pdf --pages . 6-10 -- pages6-10.pdf\n   211\t\n   212\t# Rotate pages\n   213\tqpdf input.pdf output.pdf --rotate=+90:1  # Rotate page 1 by 90 degrees\n   214\t\n   215\t# Remove password\n   216\tqpdf --password=mypassword --decrypt encrypted.pdf decrypted.pdf\n   217\t```\n   218\t\n   219\t### pdftk (if available)\n   220\t```bash\n   221\t# Merge\n   222\tpdftk file1.pdf file2.pdf cat output merged.pdf\n   223\t\n   224\t# Split\n   225\tpdftk input.pdf burst\n   226\t\n   227\t# Rotate\n   228\tpdftk input.pdf rotate 1east output rotated.pdf\n   229\t```\n   230\t\n   231\t## Common Tasks\n   232\t\n   233\t### Extract Text from Scanned PDFs\n   234\t```python\n   235\t# Requires: pip install pytesseract pdf2image\n   236\timport pytesseract\n   237\tfrom pdf2image import convert_from_path\n   238\t\n   239\t# Convert PDF to images\n   240\timages = convert_from_path('scanned.pdf')\n   241\t\n   242\t# OCR each page\n   243\ttext = \"\"\n   244\tfor i, image in enumerate(images):\n   245\t    text += f\"Page {i+1}:\\n\"\n   246\t    text += pytesseract.image_to_string(image)\n   247\t    text += \"\\n\\n\"\n   248\t\n   249\tprint(text)\n   250\t```\n   251\t\n   252\t### Add Watermark\n   253\t```python\n   254\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter\n   255\t\n   256\t# Create watermark (or load existing)\n   257\twatermark = PdfReader(\"watermark.pdf\").pages[0]\n   258\t\n   259\t# Apply to all pages\n   260\treader = PdfReader(\"document.pdf\")\n   261\twriter = PdfWriter()\n   262\t\n   263\tfor page in reader.pages:\n   264\t    page.merge_page(watermark)\n   265\t    writer.add_page(page)\n   266\t\n   267\twith open(\"watermarked.pdf\", \"wb\") as output:\n   268\t    writer.write(output)\n   269\t```\n   270\t\n   271\t### Extract Images\n   272\t```bash\n   273\t# Using pdfimages (poppler-utils)\n   274\tpdfimages -j input.pdf output_prefix\n   275\t\n   276\t# This extracts all images as output_prefix-000.jpg, output_prefix-001.jpg, etc.\n   277\t```\n   278\t\n   279\t### Password Protection\n   280\t```python\n   281\tfrom pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter\n   282\t\n   283\treader = PdfReader(\"input.pdf\")\n   284\twriter = PdfWriter()\n   285\t\n   286\tfor page in reader.pages:\n   287\t    writer.add_page(page)\n   288\t\n   289\t# Add password\n   290\twriter.encrypt(\"userpassword\", \"ownerpassword\")\n   291\t\n   292\twith open(\"encrypted.pdf\", \"wb\") as output:\n   293\t    writer.write(output)\n   294\t```\n   295\t\n   296\t## Quick Reference\n   297\t\n   298\t| Task | Best Tool | Command/Code |\n   299\t|------|-----------|--------------|\n   300\t| Merge PDFs | pypdf | `writer.add_page(page)` |\n   301\t| Split PDFs | pypdf | One page per file |\n   302\t| Extract text | pdfplumber | `page.extract_text()` |\n   303\t| Extract tables | pdfplumber | `page.extract_tables()` |\n   304\t| Create PDFs | reportlab | Canvas or Platypus |\n   305\t| Command line merge | qpdf | `qpdf --empty --pages ...` |\n   306\t| OCR scanned PDFs | pytesseract | Convert to image first |\n   307\t| Fill PDF forms | pdf-lib or pypdf (see FORMS.md) | See FORMS.md |\n   308\t\n   309\t## Next Steps\n   310\t\n   311\t- For advanced pypdfium2 usage, see REFERENCE.md\n   312\t- For JavaScript libraries (pdf-lib), see REFERENCE.md\n   313\t- If you need to fill out a PDF form, follow the instructions in FORMS.md\n   314\t- For troubleshooting guides, see REFERENCE.md\n   315\t",

                            }

                            "file_text": "from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import A4\nfrom reportlab.lib.units import mm\nfrom reportlab.lib import colors\nfrom reportlab.platypus import (\n    SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, Table, TableStyle, HRFlowable\n)\nfrom reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet, ParagraphStyle\nfrom reportlab.lib.enums import TA_LEFT, TA_RIGHT, TA_CENTER\n\n# ---------- Styles ----------\nstyles = getSampleStyleSheet()\n\nnavy = colors.HexColor(\"#1a2b4c\")\ngray = colors.HexColor(\"#5a6472\")\nlightgray = colors.HexColor(\"#f2f4f7\")\naccent = colors.HexColor(\"#2f6fed\")\nlinegray = colors.HexColor(\"#d9dee5\")\n\ntitle_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"TitleStyle\", parent=styles[\"Title\"], fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\",\n    fontSize=20, textColor=navy, spaceAfter=2, alignment=TA_LEFT\n)\nsubtitle_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"SubtitleStyle\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica\",\n    fontSize=10.5, textColor=gray, spaceAfter=14, alignment=TA_LEFT\n)\nsection_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"SectionStyle\", parent=styles[\"Heading2\"], fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\",\n    fontSize=12.5, textColor=navy, spaceBefore=16, spaceAfter=6\n)\nnote_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"NoteStyle\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica-Oblique\",\n    fontSize=8.7, textColor=gray, spaceBefore=4, spaceAfter=2, leading=12\n)\nbody_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"BodyStyle\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica\",\n    fontSize=9.3, textColor=colors.black, leading=13\n)\nsmall_center = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"SmallCenter\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica\",\n    fontSize=8.5, textColor=gray, alignment=TA_CENTER\n)\n\ndef cell(text, bold=False, color=colors.black, size=9.3, align=TA_LEFT):\n    st = ParagraphStyle(\n        \"cell\", fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\" if bold else \"Helvetica\",\n        fontSize=size, textColor=color, leading=12, alignment=align\n    )\n    return Paragraph(text, st)\n\ndoc = SimpleDocTemplate(\n    \"/home/claude/woocommerce_project_pricing.pdf\",\n    pagesize=A4,\n    leftMargin=18 * mm, rightMargin=18 * mm,\n    topMargin=16 * mm, bottomMargin=16 * mm,\n)\n\nstory = []\n\n# ---------- Header ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"WooCommerce Store \u2014 Project Pricing\", title_style))\nstory.append(Paragraph(\n    \"Setup scope, licensing, and service costs for a custom WooCommerce build on existing hosting &amp; domain.\",\n    subtitle_style\n))\nstory.append(HRFlowable(width=\"100%\", thickness=1, color=linegray, spaceAfter=10))\n\n# ---------- Already Owned ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Already in Place (Client-Provided)\", section_style))\nowned_data = [\n    [cell(\"Item\", bold=True, color=colors.white), cell(\"Status\", bold=True, color=colors.white)],\n    [cell(\"Domain\"), cell(\"Provided by client\")],\n    [cell(\"Hosting\"), cell(\"WooCommerce-dedicated hosting (Hostinger) \u2014 provided by client\")],\n]\nowned_table = Table(owned_data, colWidths=[85 * mm, 85 * mm])\nowned_table.setStyle(TableStyle([\n    (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), navy),\n    (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, 1), (-1, -1), lightgray),\n    (\"GRID\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 0.5, linegray),\n    (\"VALIGN\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), \"MIDDLE\"),\n    (\"TOPPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 6),\n    (\"BOTTOMPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 6),\n    (\"LEFTPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 8),\n]))\nstory.append(owned_table)\n\n# ---------- Annual Licenses (USD) ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Annual Licenses &amp; Subscriptions \u2014 USD\", section_style))\nusd_data = [\n    [cell(\"Item\", bold=True, color=colors.white), cell(\"Purpose\", bold=True, color=colors.white),\n     cell(\"Cost / Year\", bold=True, color=colors.white, align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Elementor Pro\"), cell(\"Page building &amp; custom design\"), cell(\"$180.00\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Wordfence Premium\"), cell(\"Security &amp; firewall\"), cell(\"$149.00\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Yoast SEO Premium\"), cell(\"On-site SEO\"), cell(\"$178.80\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"CartFlows\"), cell(\"Custom checkout &amp; funnel builder\"), cell(\"$199.00\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n]\nusd_total = 180.00 + 149.00 + 178.80 + 199.00\nusd_data.append([cell(\"\", ), cell(\"Subtotal (USD/year)\", bold=True),\n                  cell(f\"${usd_total:,.2f}\", bold=True, align=TA_RIGHT)])\n\nusd_table = Table(usd_data, colWidths=[42 * mm, 88 * mm, 40 * mm])\nusd_table.setStyle(TableStyle([\n    (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), navy),\n    (\"ROWBACKGROUNDS\", (0, 1), (-1, -2), [colors.white, lightgray]),\n    (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, -1), (-1, -1), colors.HexColor(\"#e7edfb\")),\n    (\"GRID\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 0.5, linegray),\n    (\"VALIGN\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), \"MIDDLE\"),\n    (\"TOPPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 6),\n    (\"BOTTOMPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 6),\n    (\"LEFTPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 8),\n    (\"SPAN\", (0, -1), (1, -1)),\n]))\nstory.append(usd_table)\n\n# ---------- Annual Costs (INR) ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Annual Platform Costs \u2014 INR\", section_style))\ninr_data = [\n
