                            "old_str": "from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import A4\nfrom reportlab.lib.units import mm\nfrom reportlab.lib import colors\nfrom reportlab.platypus import (\n    SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, Table, TableStyle, HRFlowable\n)\nfrom reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet, ParagraphStyle\nfrom reportlab.lib.enums import TA_LEFT, TA_RIGHT, TA_CENTER\n\n# ---------- Styles ----------\nstyles = getSampleStyleSheet()",

                            "old_str": "title_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"TitleStyle\", parent=styles[\"Title\"], fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\",\n    fontSize=20, textColor=navy, spaceAfter=2, alignment=TA_LEFT\n)\nsubtitle_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"SubtitleStyle\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica\",\n    fontSize=10.5, textColor=gray, spaceAfter=14, alignment=TA_LEFT\n)\nsection_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"SectionStyle\", parent=styles[\"Heading2\"], fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\",\n    fontSize=12.5, textColor=navy, spaceBefore=16, spaceAfter=6\n)\nnote_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"NoteStyle\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica-Oblique\",\n    fontSize=8.7, textColor=gray, spaceBefore=4, spaceAfter=2, leading=12\n)\nbody_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"BodyStyle\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica\",\n    fontSize=9.3, textColor=colors.black, leading=13\n)\nsmall_center = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"SmallCenter\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica\",\n    fontSize=8.5, textColor=gray, alignment=TA_CENTER\n)\n\ndef cell(text, bold=False, color=colors.black, size=9.3, align=TA_LEFT):\n    st = ParagraphStyle(\n        \"cell\", fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\" if bold else \"Helvetica\",\n        fontSize=size, textColor=color, leading=12, alignment=align\n    )\n    return Paragraph(text, st)\n\ndoc = SimpleDocTemplate(\n    \"/home/claude/woocommerce_project_pricing.pdf\",\n    pagesize=A4,\n    leftMargin=18 * mm, rightMargin=18 * mm,\n    topMargin=16 * mm, bottomMargin=16 * mm,\n)",
                            "new_str": "title_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"TitleStyle\", parent=styles[\"Title\"], fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\",\n    fontSize=15, textColor=navy, spaceAfter=2, alignment=TA_LEFT, leading=18\n)\nsubtitle_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"SubtitleStyle\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica\",\n    fontSize=8.5, textColor=gray, spaceAfter=12, alignment=TA_LEFT, leading=11.5\n)\nsection_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"SectionStyle\", parent=styles[\"Heading2\"], fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\",\n    fontSize=10.5, textColor=navy, spaceBefore=14, spaceAfter=5, leading=13\n)\nnote_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"NoteStyle\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica-Oblique\",\n    fontSize=7.3, textColor=gray, spaceBefore=4, spaceAfter=2, leading=10\n)\nbody_style = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"BodyStyle\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica\",\n    fontSize=8, textColor=colors.black, leading=11\n)\nsmall_center = ParagraphStyle(\n    \"SmallCenter\", parent=styles[\"Normal\"], fontName=\"Helvetica\",\n    fontSize=7, textColor=gray, alignment=TA_CENTER\n)\n\ndef cell(text, bold=False, color=colors.black, size=7.8, align=TA_LEFT):\n    st = ParagraphStyle(\n        \"cell\", fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\" if bold else \"Helvetica\",\n        fontSize=size, textColor=color, leading=10.5, alignment=align\n    )\n    return Paragraph(text, st)\n\ndoc = SimpleDocTemplate(\n    \"/home/claude/woocommerce_project_pricing.pdf\",\n    pagesize=MOBILE_PAGE,\n    leftMargin=7 * mm, rightMargin=7 * mm,\n    topMargin=9 * mm, bottomMargin=9 * mm,\n)"

                            "path": "/home/claude/build_pricing_pdf.py",
                            "old_str": "story = []\n\n# ---------- Header ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"WooCommerce Store \u2014 Project Pricing\", title_style))\nstory.append(Paragraph(\n    \"Setup scope, licensing, and service costs for a custom WooCommerce build on existing hosting &amp; domain.\",\n    subtitle_style\n))\nstory.append(HRFlowable(width=\"100%\", thickness=1, color=linegray, spaceAfter=10))\n\n# ---------- Already Owned ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Already in Place (Client-Provided)\", section_style))\nowned_data = [\n    [cell(\"Item\", bold=True, color=colors.white), cell(\"Status\", bold=True, color=colors.white)],\n    [cell(\"Domain\"), cell(\"Provided by client\")],\n    [cell(\"Hosting\"), cell(\"WooCommerce-dedicated hosting (Hostinger) \u2014 provided by client\")],\n]\nowned_table = Table(owned_data, colWidths=[85 * mm, 85 * mm])\nowned_table.setStyle(TableStyle([\n    (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), navy),\n    (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, 1), (-1, -1), lightgray),\n    (\"GRID\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 0.5, linegray),\n    (\"VALIGN\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), \"MIDDLE\"),\n    (\"TOPPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 6),\n    (\"BOTTOMPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 6),\n    (\"LEFTPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 8),\n]))\nstory.append(owned_table)\n\n# ---------- Annual Licenses (USD) ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Annual Licenses &amp; Subscriptions \u2014 USD\", section_style))\nusd_data = [\n    [cell(\"Item\", bold=True, color=colors.white), cell(\"Purpose\", bold=True, color=colors.white),\n     cell(\"Cost / Year\", bold=True, color=colors.white, align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Elementor Pro\"), cell(\"Page building &amp; custom design\"), cell(\"$180.00\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Wordfence Premium\"), cell(\"Security &amp; firewall\"), cell(\"$149.00\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Yoast SEO Premium\"), cell(\"On-site SEO\"), cell(\"$178.80\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"CartFlows\"), cell(\"Custom checkout &amp; funnel builder\"), cell(\"$199.00\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n]\nusd_total = 180.00 + 149.00 + 178.80 + 199.00\nusd_data.append([cell(\"\", ), cell(\"Subtotal (USD/year)\", bold=True),\n                  cell(f\"${usd_total:,.2f}\", bold=True, align=TA_RIGHT)])\n\nusd_table = Table(usd_data, colWidths=[42 * mm, 88 * mm, 40 * mm])\nusd_table.setStyle(TableStyle([\n    (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), navy),\n    (\"ROWBACKGROUNDS\", (0, 1), (-1, -2), [colors.white, lightgray]),\n    (\"BACKGROUND\", (0, -1), (-1, -1), colors.HexColor(\"#e7edfb\")),\n    (\"GRID\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 0.5, linegray),\n    (\"VALIGN\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), \"MIDDLE\"),\n    (\"TOPPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 6),\n    (\"BOTTOMPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 6),\n    (\"LEFTPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 8),\n    (\"SPAN\", (0, -1), (1, -1)),\n]))\nstory.append(usd_table)\n\n# ---------- Annual Costs (INR) ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Annual Platform Costs \u2014 INR\", section_style))\ninr_data = [\n    [cell(\"Item\", bold=True, color=colors.white), cell(\"Purpose\", bold=True, color=colors.white),\n     cell(\"Cost / Year\", bold=True, color=colors.white, align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Shiprocket\"), cell(\"Shipping &amp; Convert at the time of invoicing if a single-currency \"\n    \"quote is needed for the client.\",\n    note_style\n))\n\nstory.append(Paragraph(\n    \"Usage-based costs not included in totals above: Razorpay transaction fees (~2\u20132.5% per transaction) \"\n    \"and Shiprocket per-shipment charges. These scale with order volume and are billed by the respective platforms.\",\n    note_style\n))\n\nstory.append(Spacer(1, 14))\nstory.append(HRFlowable(width=\"100%\", thickness=0.75, color=linegray))\nstory.append(Spacer(1, 4))\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Prepared as a working cost reference \u2014 confirm final vendor pricing before invoicing the client.\", small_center))\n\ndoc.build(story)\nprint(\"PDF created.\")",
                            "new_str": "story = []\n\nFULL_W = PAGE_WIDTH - 14 * mm  # usable content width after margins\nLABEL_W = FULL_W * 0.60\nVALUE_W = FULL_W * 0.40\n\ndef two_col_table(rows, header=True, highlight_last=False):\n    t = Table(rows, colWidths=[LABEL_W, VALUE_W])\n    style = [\n        (\"GRID\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 0.5, linegray),\n        (\"VALIGN\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), \"TOP\"),\n        (\"TOPPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 5),\n        (\"BOTTOMPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 5),\n        (\"LEFTPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 5),\n        (\"RIGHTPADDING\", (0, 0), (-1, -1), 5),\n    ]\n    start = 0\n    if header:\n        style.append((\"BACKGROUND\", (0, 0), (-1, 0), navy))\n        start = 1\n    body_end = len(rows) - 1 if highlight_last else len(rows) - 1\n    style.append((\"ROWBACKGROUNDS\", (0, start), (-1, body_end if not highlight_last else body_end - 1),\n                   [colors.white, lightgray]))\n    if highlight_last:\n        style.append((\"BACKGROUND\", (0, -1), (-1, -1), colors.HexColor(\"#e7edfb\")))\n    t.setStyle(TableStyle(style))\n    return t\n\n# ---------- Header ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"WooCommerce Store \u2014 Pricing\", title_style))\nstory.append(Paragraph(\n    \"Licensing &amp; platform costs for a custom WooCommerce build on existing hosting &amp; domain. All figures in USD.\",\n    subtitle_style\n))\nstory.append(HRFlowable(width=\"100%\", thickness=1, color=linegray, spaceAfter=8))\n\n# ---------- Already Owned ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Already in Place\", section_style))\nowned_rows = [\n    [cell(\"Item\", bold=True, color=colors.white), cell(\"Status\", bold=True, color=colors.white)],\n    [cell(\"Domain\"), cell(\"Provided by client\")],\n    [cell(\"Hosting\"), cell(\"WooCommerce hosting (Hostinger) \u2014 provided by client\")],\n]\nstory.append(two_col_table(owned_rows))\n\n# ---------- Annual Licenses (USD) ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Annual Licenses &amp; Subscriptions\", section_style))\nusd_rows = [\n    [cell(\"Item\", bold=True, color=colors.white), cell(\"Cost / Year\", bold=True, color=colors.white, align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Elementor Pro\"), cell(\"$180.00\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Wordfence Premium\"), cell(\"$149.00\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Yoast SEO Premium\"), cell(\"$178.80\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"CartFlows\"), cell(\"$199.00\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n]\n\n# Shiprocket converted to USD for a single-currency sheet\nusd_inr_rate = 95.43\nshiprocket_inr = 2400.00\nshiprocket_usd = shiprocket_inr / usd_inr_rate\nusd_rows.append([cell(\"Shiprocket (starter plan)\"), cell(f\"${shiprocket_usd:,.2f}\", align=TA_RIGHT)])\n\nusd_total = 180.00 + 149.00 + 178.80 + 199.00 + shiprocket_usd\nusd_rows.append([cell(\"Total / Year\", bold=True), cell(f\"${usd_total:,.2f}\", bold=True, align=TA_RIGHT)])\n\nstory.append(two_col_table(usd_rows, highlight_last=True))\nstory.append(Paragraph(\n    f\"Shiprocket is billed in INR (\u20b92,400/year); converted here at \u2248\u20b9{usd_inr_rate:.2f}/USD for a \"\n    \"single-currency reference. Actual charge will follow live INR pricing at time of billing.\",\n    note_style\n))\nstory.append(Paragraph(\n    \"Not included above \u2014 usage-based fees: Razorpay ~2\u20132.5% per transaction, \"\n    \"Shiprocket per-shipment charges. Both scale with order volume.\",\n    note_style\n))\n\n# ---------- Free / Included ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Included at No Extra Cost\", section_style))\nfree_rows = [\n    [cell(\"Item\", bold=True, color=colors.white), cell(\"Notes\", bold=True, color=colors.white)],\n    [cell(\"LiteSpeed Cache\"), cell(\"Free with Hostinger \u2014 site speed/caching\")],\n    [cell(\"SSL Certificate\"), cell(\"Free with Hostinger hosting plan\")],\n    [cell(\"Tax Calculation\"), cell(\"WooCommerce's built-in tax settings \u2014 no external plugin needed\")],\n    [cell(\"WP Mail SMTP + Brevo\"), cell(\"Free tier \u2014 300 emails/day for order confirmations\")],\n]\nstory.append(two_col_table(free_rows))\n\n# ---------- Optional / As-needed ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Optional Add-Ons (Not in Total)\", section_style))\nopt_rows = [\n    [cell(\"Item\", bold=True, color=colors.white), cell(\"Why\", bold=True, color=colors.white)],\n    [cell(\"UpdraftPlus Premium (~$70/yr)\"), cell(\"Off-site backups \u2014 recommended\")],\n    [cell(\"Abandoned Cart Recovery\"), cell(\"Upsell once store has traffic\")],\n    [cell(\"PDF Invoices &amp; Packing Slips\"), cell(\"Free plugin \u2014 needed for GST invoices\")],\n    [cell(\"Legal Pages\"), cell(\"Privacy/Refund/Terms \u2014 reviewed for compliance\")],\n]\nstory.append(two_col_table(opt_rows))\n\n# ---------- Summary ----------\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Cost Summary\", section_style))\nsummary_rows = [\n    [cell(\"Category\", bold=True, color=colors.white), cell(\"Amount\", bold=True, color=colors.white, align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Elementor + Wordfence + Yoast + CartFlows\"), cell(\"$706.80 / yr\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Shiprocket (converted)\"), cell(f\"${shiprocket_usd:,.2f} / yr\", align=TA_RIGHT)],\n    [cell(\"Total Annual Cost\", bold=True), cell(f\"${usd_total:,.2f} / yr\", bold=True, align=TA_RIGHT)],\n]\nstory.append(two_col_table(summary_rows, highlight_last=True))\n\nstory.append(Spacer(1, 10))\nstory.append(HRFlowable(width=\"100%\", thickness=0.75, color=linegray))\nstory.append(Spacer(1, 4))\nstory.append(Paragraph(\"Working cost reference \u2014 confirm live vendor pricing &amp; exchange rate before invoicing.\", small_center))\n\ndoc.build(story)\nprint(\"PDF created.\")"

                                "type": "knowledge",
                                "title": "Vithal Rao",
                                "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vithal_Rao",
                                "metadata": {
                                    "type": "webpage_metadata",
                                    "site_domain": "wikipedia.org",

                                    "site_name": "Wikipedia"
                                },
                                "is_missing": false,
                                "text": "- Born: 1929\n- Origin: Hyderabad, Telangana, India\n- Died: 25 June 2015 (aged 85\u201386) Hyderabad\n- Genres: Ghazals\n- Occupation: singer\n- Years active: 1920s \u2013 2015\n\nVithal Rao Aatmaram Shivpurkar also known as Pandit Vithal Rao (1929 \u2013 25 June 2015) was an Indian classical vocalist.\n[[1]](./Vithal_Rao#cite_note-1) He is considered to have been one of the last few maestros of 'Ghazals' and was the last court singer of the [Last Nizam of Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osman_Ali_Khan). He is popular for singing ghazals written by Hyderabad writers, [[2]](./Vithal_Rao#cite_note-canada.com-2) Urdu as well as Hindi ghazals.\nVithal Rao Aatmaram Shivpurkar popularly known as Vithal Rao or Guruji was born in 1929 in\n[Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyderabad), India.\nVithal Rao was a court musician of the Last Nizam of Hyderabad. He is popular with the noble families of Hyderabad. Among his notable accompanists include Ustad Sardar Khan (tabla).\n\n\nSome of his notable disciples include Kiran Ahluwalia, playback and folk singer\n[Rahul Sipligunj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahul_Sipligunj), and ghazal and qawwali singer [Chetan Ramlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chetan_Ramlu).\nSome his popular ghazals are:\n- Ek chamele ke mandve tale\n- Maine teri aaakho mein pada, sab bhool gaya bus yaad raha sirf Allah hi Allah (Sufiana Kalam)\n- Aye mere ham nasheen\n- Nindiya Na Aaye\n- O Sitamgar Tera Muskurana Kaam Aane Ke Qabil Nahin Hai\n- Zehal-e miskin makun taghaful, duraye naina banaye batiyan\n[[3]](./Vithal_Rao#cite_note-3)\n- Ghazals of Janab Sayeed Shaheedi\n[\u2191](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vithal_Rao#cite_ref-1) [\"Legendary Ghazal singer Vithal Rao dead\"](http://www.thehindu.com/news/national/telangana/legendary-ghazal-singer-vithal-rao-dead/article7358221.ece). [The Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hindu). 26 June 2015. Retrieved 26 June 2015.",

                                "type": "knowledge",
                                "title": "Vrajlal Shastri",
                                "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vrajlal_Shastri",
                                "metadata": {
                                    "type": "webpage_metadata",
                                    "site_domain": "wikipedia.org",
                                    "favicon_url": "https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?sz=64&domain=wikipedia.org",
                                    "site_name": "Wikipedia"
                                },
                                "is_missing": false,
                                "text": "- Born: Vrajlal Kalidas Shastri 26 November 1825 Malataj, Petlad, Gujarat\n- Died: 14 November 1892 (aged 66)\n- Occupation: philologist, poet, scholar, translator\n- Language: Gujarati\n- Nationality: Indian\n- Notable works: Gujarati Bhashano Itihas (1866); Utsargmala (1870); Dhatusangrah (1870); Gurjar Bhasha Prakash (1892);\n\nVrajlal Kalidas Shastri (26 November 1825 \u2013 14 November 1892) was a pioneer of\n[philology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philology) in [Gujarati language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarati_language) and writer who wrote books on Gujarati language. [[2]](./Vrajlal_Shastri#cite_note-Lal1992-2) [[3]](./Vrajlal_Shastri#cite_note-Sebeok1972-3)\nHe was born on 26 November 1825 in Malataj, a village in\n[Petlad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petlad), [Gujarat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarat). He took his primary education in his village Malataj. [[1]](./Vrajlal_Shastri#cite_note-vs-1) He had studied Sanskrit poetry and grammar in Sanskrit pathshalas. \n\nHe also studied [Prakrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prakrit_language) grammar and literature. He taught Sanskrit at Jain Mandir, [Ahmedabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmedabad). Due to Jain religious books, he became familiar with Prakrit- [Pali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pali), [Apabhramsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apabhramsa), [Ardha Magadhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magadhi_Prakrit) so he wrote books on philology of Gujarati. He worked with [Gujarat Vernacular Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gujarat_Vidhya_Sabha) and Dharma Sabha and edited their two journals, [Buddhiprakash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhiprakash) and Dharmaprakash. He had a long career of twenty five years as a researcher and scholar. [[2]](./Vrajlal_Shastri#cite_note-Lal1992-2) [[4]](./Vrajlal_Shastri#cite_note-Mukherjee1998-4)\nHe died on 14 November 1892.\n[[1]](./Vrajlal_Shastri#cite_note-vs-1)\nHe had written fifteen books. \n\nHis books on Gujarati language include Gujarati Bhashano Iti",
                                "is_citable": true,

                                "type": "knowledge",
                                "title": "Vraj Hindu Temple",
                                "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vraj_Hindu_Temple",
                                "metadata": {
                                    "type": "webpage_metadata",
                                    "site_domain": "wikipedia.org",
                                    "favicon_url": "https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?sz=64&domain=wikipedia.org",
                                    "site_name": "Wikipedia"
                                },
                                "is_missing": false,
                                "text": "This article needs additional citations for\nThe Vraj Hindu Temple is located at 51 Manor Road in\n[Schuylkill Haven, Pennsylvania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schuylkill_Haven,_Pennsylvania), two miles west of the intersection of Pennsylvania Routes [183](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Route_183) and [895](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvania_Route_895).\nThe temple is a multimillion-dollar temple or haveli covering 100 acres (0.40 km2) of the land. Vraj is also known as Nootan Nandalay, and abode for God\n[Shrinathji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrinathji), a manifestation of [Krishna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krishna). It is visited by an average of 100,000 [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu) pilgrims annually. [[1]](./Vraj_Hindu_Temple#cite_note-1) Vraj offers daily prayers (Darshans), annual events and volunteer opportunities for young people.\nThe temple is associated with the\n\n\n[Pushtimarg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pushtimarg) [Vaishnav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaishnavism) sect. [[2]](./Vraj_Hindu_Temple#cite_note-2) [[3]](./Vraj_Hindu_Temple#cite_note-Bryant2007p477-3)\nBefore 1987, Vraj\n[Hindu Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_temple) was a yoga recreational center. The land was purchased in the summer of 1987 by the temple's visionary founder Govind Bhikhabhai Shah (Kaka) with support from 29 other investors. The temple became official in November 1988 during the inaugural Patotsav Celebration when it was claimed that [Shrinathji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrinathji) appeared for the first time at Vraj.[ [citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed)]\nThe Temple has daily darshans. Darshan is a sanctum that is organized six times a day (everyday) as different prayers. Each Darshan has a special mood, emotion, clothes, music and food:[\n\n\n[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed)]\n- Mangala - to underline the auspiciousness of the beginning",

                                "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vrajesh_Hirjee",
                                "metadata": {

                                    "site_name": "Wikipedia"
                                },
                                "is_missing": false,
                                "text": "- Born: 16 June 1971 (age 54) Mumbai, Maharashtra, India\n- Occupations: Actor TV presenter Comedian Script writer Dubbing artist\n- Years active: 1995\u2013present\n- Spouse: Rohini Banerjee \u200b(m. 2015)\u200b\n\n![]()\nThis\nVrajesh Hirjee (born 16 June 1971) is an Indian film and television actor and comedian. [[4]](./Vrajesh_Hirjee#cite_note-4)\nVrajesh was born and grew up in\n[Kalbadevi, Mumbai, India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalbadevi).                                 "is_missing": false,
                                "text": "- Born: 1929\n- Origin: Hyderabad, Telangana, India\n- Died: 25 June 2015 (aged 85\u201386) Hyderabad\n- Genres: Ghazals\n- Occupation: singer\n- Years active: 1920s \u2013 2015\n\nVithal Rao Aatmaram Shivpurkar also known as Pandit Vithal Rao (1929 \u2013 25 June 2015) was an Indian classical vocalist.\n[[1]](./Vithal_Rao#cite_note-1) He is considered to have been one of the last few maestros of 'Ghazals' and was the last court singer of the [Last Nizam of Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osman_Ali_Khan). He is popular for singing ghazals written by Hyderabad writers, [[2]](./Vithal_Rao#cite_note-canada.com-2) Urdu as well as Hindi ghazals.\nVithal Rao Aatmaram Shivpurkar popularly known as Vithal Rao or Guruji was born in 1929 in\n[Hyderabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyderabad), India.\nVithal Rao was a court musician of the Last Nizam of Hyderabad. He is popular with the noble families of Hyderabad. Among his notable accompanists include Ustad Sardar Khan (tabla).\n\n\nSome of his notable disciples include Kiran Ahluwalia, playback and folk singer\n[Rahul Sipligunj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahul_Sipligunj), and ghazal and qawwali singer [Chetan Ramlu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chetan_Ramlu).\nSome his popular ghazals are:\n- Ek chamele ke mandve tale\n- Maine teri aaakho mein pada, sab bhool gaya bus yaad raha sirf Allah hi Allah (Sufiana Kalam)\n- Aye mere ham nasheen\n- Nindiya Na Aaye\n- O Sitamgar Tera Muskurana Kaam Aane Ke Qabil Nahin Hai\n- Zehal-e miskin makun taghaful, duraye naina banaye batiyan\n[[3]](./Vithal_Rao#cite_note-3)\n- Ghazals of Janab Sayeed Shaheedi\n[\u2191](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vithal_Rao#cite_ref-1) [\"Legendary Ghazal singer Vithal Rao dead\"](http://www.thehindu.com/news/national/telangana/legendary-ghazal-singer-vithal-rao-dead/article7358221.ece). [The Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hindu). 26 June 2015. Retrieved 26 June 2015.",

                                "is_missing": false,
                                "text": "[Pushtimarg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pushtimarg) [Vaishnav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaishnavism) sect. [[2]](./Vraj_Hindu_Temple#cite_note-2) [[3]](./Vraj_Hindu_Temple#cite_note-Bryant2007p477-3)\nBefore 1987, Vraj\n[Hindu Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu_temple) was a yoga recreational center. The land was purchased in the summer of 1987 by the temple's visionary founder Govind Bhikhabhai Shah (Kaka) with support from 29 other investors. The temple became official in November 1988 during the inaugural Patotsav Celebration when it was claimed that [Shrinathji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrinathji) appeared for the first time at Vraj.[ [citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed)]\nThe Temple has daily darshans. Darshan is a sanctum that is organized six times a day (everyday) as different prayers. Each Darshan has a special mood, emotion, clothes, music and food:[\n\n\n[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed)]\n- Mangala - to underline the auspiciousness of the beginning the day.\n- Shringar -\n[Shrinathji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrinathji)is dressed, and a flower garland is placed around his neck.\n- Raajbhoog - the main meal of the day with different types of sweet.\n- Utthapan - for when\n[Shrinathji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shrinathji)is believed to wake up from afternoon siesta.\n- Sandhya Arati - an evening prayer with light garland.\n- Shayan - a celebration with music and drumming to herald the final prayer of the day.\nVraj contains a youth group consisting of young volunteers and students from all over the United States. It was created to encourage service to the community and broaden knowledge of Indian Heritage.\n[[4]](./Vraj_Hindu_Temple#cite_note-4) Vraj youth members are mostly in high school, college, graduate school, or working with ages ranging anywhere from 16 to 28.\n\n\nVraj youth are involved in all occasions offered from the temple such as prayers, food, fest",

                            "new_str": "from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import portrait\nfrom reportlab.lib.units import mm\nfrom reportlab.lib import colors\nfrom reportlab.platypus import (\n    SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, Table, TableStyle, HRFlowable\n)\nfrom reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet, ParagraphStyle\nfrom reportlab.lib.enums import TA_LEFT, TA_RIGHT, TA_CENTER\nfrom reportlab.pdfgen import canvas as pdfcanvas\n\n# Narrow \"mobile\" page size (like a phone-width doc) so it reads cleanly\n# on a phone screen without side-scrolling or tiny zoomed text.\nPAGE_WIDTH = 100 * mm\nPAGE_HEIGHT = 210 * mm\nMOBILE_PAGE = (PAGE_WIDTH, PAGE_HEIGHT)\n\nCONTACT_NAME = \"Vraj Vithalani\"\nCONTACT_PHONE = \"+91 84604 74721\"\nCONTACT_WEBSITE = (\"vrajvithalani.com\", \"https://vrajvithalani.com/\")\nCONTACT_LINKEDIN = (\"LinkedIn\", \"https://in.linkedin.com/in/vraj-vithalani-508890165\")\n\n# ---------- Styles ----------\nstyles = getSampleStyleSheet()",
                            "old_str": "from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import portrait\nfrom reportlab.lib.units import mm\nfrom reportlab.lib import colors\nfrom reportlab.platypus import (\n    SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, Table, TableStyle, HRFlowable\n)\nfrom reportlab.lib.styles import getSampleStyleSheet, ParagraphStyle\nfrom reportlab.lib.enums import TA_LEFT, TA_RIGHT, TA_CENTER\n\n# Narrow \"mobile\" page size (like a phone-width doc) so it reads cleanly\n# on a phone screen without side-scrolling or tiny zoomed text.\nPAGE_WIDTH = 100 * mm\nPAGE_HEIGHT = 210 * mm\nMOBILE_PAGE = (PAGE_WIDTH, PAGE_HEIGHT)\n\n# ---------- Styles ----------\nstyles = getSampleStyleSheet()",

                        "name": "str_replace",
                        "input": {
                            "new_str": "def cell(text, bold=False, color=colors.black, size=7.8, align=TA_LEFT):\n    st = ParagraphStyle(\n        \"cell\", fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\" if bold else \"Helvetica\",\n        fontSize=size, textColor=color, leading=10.5, alignment=align\n    )\n    return Paragraph(text, st)\n\n\ndef draw_watermark_and_footer(c: pdfcanvas.Canvas, doc):\n    c.saveState()\n\n    # ---- Diagonal watermark, 45 degrees, subtle ----\n    c.setFont(\"Helvetica-Bold\", 15)\n    c.setFillColor(colors.Color(0.6, 0.65, 0.72, alpha=0.16))\n    c.translate(PAGE_WIDTH / 2, PAGE_HEIGHT / 2)\n    c.rotate(45)\n    # repeat the watermark a few times down the diagonal so it covers the page\n    for offset in range(-3, 4):\n        c.drawCentredString(0, offset * 34, \"Vraj Vithalani\")\n    c.restoreState()\n\n    # ---- Footer band: initials + contact + clickable social links ----\n    c.saveState()\n    footer_h = 15 * mm\n    c.setFillColor(navy)\n    c.rect(0, 0, PAGE_WIDTH, footer_h, fill=1, stroke=0)\n\n    c.setFillColor(colors.white)\n    c.setFont(\"Helvetica-Bold\", 8)\n    c.drawString(6 * mm, footer_h - 5.2 * mm, \"VV\")\n    c.setFont(\"Helvetica-Bold\", 7.2)\n    c.drawString(12 * mm, footer_h - 5.2 * mm, CONTACT_NAME)\n\n    c.setFont(\"Helvetica\", 6.4)\n    c.setFillColor(colors.HexColor(\"#cbd5e8\"))\n    c.drawString(6 * mm, footer_h - 9.8 * mm, f\"Tel: {CONTACT_PHONE}\")\n\n    # Website \u2014 tappable\n    site_label = CONTACT_WEBSITE[0]\n    site_x = 6 * mm\n    site_y = footer_h - 13.6 * mm\n    c.setFillColor(colors.HexColor(\"#8fb4ff\"))\n    c.setFont(\"Helvetica\", 6.4)\n    c.drawString(site_x, site_y, site_label)\n    site_w = c.stringWidth(site_label, \"Helvetica\", 6.4)\n    c.linkURL(CONTACT_WEBSITE[1], (site_x, site_y - 1, site_x + site_w, site_y + 6), relative=0)\n\n    # LinkedIn \u2014 tappable\n    li_label = CONTACT_LINKEDIN[0]\n    li_x = site_x + site_w + 6 * mm\n    c.setFillColor(colors.HexColor(\"#8fb4ff\"))\n    c.drawString(li_x, site_y, li_label)\n    li_w = c.stringWidth(li_label, \"Helvetica\", 6.4)\n    c.linkURL(CONTACT_LINKEDIN[1], (li_x, site_y - 1, li_x + li_w, site_y + 6), relative=0)\n\n    # phone tap-to-call\n    tel_x = 6 * mm\n    tel_y = footer_h - 9.8 * mm\n    tel_w = c.stringWidth(f\"Tel: {CONTACT_PHONE}\", \"Helvetica\", 6.4)\n    c.linkURL(f\"tel:{CONTACT_PHONE.replace(' ', '')}\", (tel_x, tel_y - 1, tel_x + tel_w, tel_y + 6), relative=0)\n\n    c.restoreState()\n\n\ndoc = SimpleDocTemplate(\n    \"/home/claude/woocommerce_project_pricing.pdf\",\n    pagesize=MOBILE_PAGE,\n    leftMargin=7 * mm, rightMargin=7 * mm,\n    topMargin=9 * mm, bottomMargin=19 * mm,\n)",
                            "old_str": "def cell(text, bold=False, color=colors.black, size=7.8, align=TA_LEFT):\n    st = ParagraphStyle(\n        \"cell\", fontName=\"Helvetica-Bold\" if bold else \"Helvetica\",\n        fontSize=size, textColor=color, leading=10.5, alignment=align\n    )\n    return Paragraph(text, st)\n\ndoc = SimpleDocTemplate(\n    \"/home/claude/woocommerce_project_pricing.pdf\",\n    pagesize=MOBILE_PAGE,\n    leftMargin=7 * mm, rightMargin=7 * mm,\n    topMargin=9 * mm, bottomMargin=9 * mm,\n)",

            {
                "uuid": "01a00a88-e973-7424-ad94-4f3e5389f94e",
                "text": "Hey hi, I want to talk about AI videos. I am actually having google one plus subscription and Claude pro too. I want to create a 30-35 second video with consistent characters and very realistic looking stuff. But I don't know how to do that. I see so many videos of people using google omni, but I am unable to do so. Can you pull me some YouTube videos that might help me master the google omni use? I want to use my own face into the video and want it to be professional.",
                "content": [
                    {
                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-16T12:25:41.151814Z",

                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "Hey hi, I want to talk about AI videos. I am actually having google one plus subscription and Claude pro too. I want to create a 30-35 second video with consistent characters and very realistic looking stuff. But I don't know how to do that. I want to use my own face into the video and want it to be professional.",

            {
                "uuid": "01a00a88-e973-70bd-b812-90ec1a115705",
                "text": "I can look into this, but let me flag a couple of things first so I search for the right thing.\n\nI think you might mean **Veo** (Google's video generation model, accessible via Gemini/Google AI) rather than \"Google Omni\" \u2014 I'm not aware of a Google product by that exact name for video. It's possible you're thinking of \"Nano Banana\" or some other nickname that's circulating, or a specific app built on Veo. Let me search to confirm what's current before sending you down the wrong path.\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nGood \u2014 \"Google Omni\" is real, you weren't misremembering. It's **Gemini Omni Flash**, Google DeepMind's newer video model (launched at I/O 2026), and it lives inside Gemini chat rather than being a separate app. A couple of important things before I pull videos for you:\n\n**What it actually does well**: it creates AI videos up to 10 seconds long with synchronized audio from text, images, audio, and video inputs, and it's built around multi-turn conversational editing, letting you refine results by chatting rather than regenerating from scratch, with strong character consistency.\n\n**The catch for your 30-35 second goal**: since each generation tops out around 10 seconds, you won't get one continuous clip that long \u2014 you'd generate multiple shots/segments and stitch them together (Gemini Omni's strength is keeping characters/scenes consistent *across* those generations, so this is very doable, just not \"one button press\").\n\n**On using your own face**: this is worth being upfront about \u2014 realistic video of your own likeness usually works by uploading a reference photo of yourself so the model can maintain your appearance across shots. That part is legitimate and commonly supported. Just flagging so you know what to expect, not because there's an issue with wanting to do it.\n\nLet me find some actual tutorial videos on this.\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nFound some real tutorials. Here's the picture, then the videos:\n\n**Access**: Since you have a Google One/AI subscription, you get Gemini Omni Flash through the **Gemini app** and **Google Flow** (Google's video editor/scene-builder tool) \u2014 that's actually the better surface for your use case since Flow is built for multi-scene, longer projects.\n\n**For your 30-35 sec video**: clips cap at 10 seconds per generation, so the real skill is generating consistent 4-5 shots and stitching them in Flow's scene builder \u2014 that's exactly what a couple of these tutorials cover.\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nA few practical tips pulled from what these guides emphasize, so you get the most out of them:\n\n- **Use one clean reference photo of yourself** \u2014 good lighting, front-facing, neutral background \u2014 and feed it in first before writing any scene prompts. This is what the model locks onto for consistency.\n- **Write your prompts in stages, not one giant paragraph**: start broad (\"me sitting at a desk talking to camera, office background\"), generate, then refine turn-by-turn (\"now have me smile,\" \"warmer lighting,\" \"add hand gesture\"). Omni is built for this conversational editing loop rather than one perfect prompt.\n- **Restate consistency details every time** you start a new segment/shot \u2014 same outfit, same lighting style, same setting \u2014 because separate generations don't automatically inherit context unless you're editing forward in the same thread.\n- **Stitch in Flow's scene builder**, not a separate video editor, since that's what keeps the character/style anchored across your 30-35 second timeline.\n- Every clip gets an invisible **SynthID watermark** for AI-provenance \u2014 expected and not removable, just so it doesn't surprise you.\n\nOne more thing worth knowing: for genuinely \"very realistic\" results, output is capped at 720p-1080p on Flash, so if you want the sharpest possible final render, generate in Omni and do a light upscale/color pass afterward \u2014 several of these guides mention that as standard practice rather than a workaround.\n\nWant me to help you actually draft the shot-by-shot prompts for your specific video once you know what it's about?",
                "content": [

                        "flags": null,
                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "Good \u2014 \"Google Omni\" is real, you weren't misremembering. It's **Gemini Omni Flash**, Google DeepMind's newer video model (launched at I/O 2026), and it lives inside Gemini chat rather than being a separate app. A couple of important things before I pull videos for you:\n\n**What it actually does well**: it creates AI videos up to 10 seconds long with synchronized audio from text, images, audio, and video inputs, and it's built around multi-turn conversational editing, letting you refine results by chatting rather than regenerating from scratch, with strong character consistency.\n\n**The catch for your 30-35 second goal**: since each generation tops out around 10 seconds, you won't get one continuous clip that long \u2014 you'd generate multiple shots/segments and stitch them together (Gemini Omni's strength is keeping characters/scenes consistent *across* those generations, so this is very doable, just not \"one button press\").\n\n**On using your own face**: this is worth being upfront about \u2014 realistic video of your own likeness usually works by uploading a reference photo of yourself so the model can maintain your appearance across shots. That part is legitimate and commonly supported. Just flagging so you know what to expect, not because there's an issue with wanting to do it.\n\nLet me find some actual tutorial videos on this.",

                                "text": "In practice we do three things in combination: First, define a Gemini Omni Character once with a clean reference image and a tight description, and pass that character_id into every Video job that should feature the character.\n\nTLDRA hands-on tutorial for video makers using Gemini Omni Flash, Google's multimodal video model from I/O 2026. In practice we do three things in combination: First, define a Gemini Omni Character once with a clean reference image and a tight description, and pass that character_id into every Video job that should feature the character. A character_ids array allows up to 3 entries per job, which is enough for a two-hander plus a recurring object. Second, lock the seed value across runs that should share a visual feel. \n\nSeeds don't guarantee identical outputs once prompts change, but they reduce drift on style and lighting.                                 "text": "... Character consistency across multiple Shorts. Upload a reference image of a character once, then use it in every generation that week. Omni Flash shifts the workflow because of one feature: conversational editing. You don\u2019t write one perfect prompt. You write a rough first prompt, then refine across three to six follow-up instructions that build on each other. Characters stay consistent. Lighting carries over. The scene \u201cremembers\u201d what came before. That changes what you can actually make. With Veo, you were rolling dice on a final shot. \n\nWith Omni Flash, you\u2019re directing \u2014 say \u201cmake the lighting warmer\u201d, then \u201chave her glance left\u201d, then \u201cadd the rain sound from this audio file\u201d, and each instruction lands on top of the last. The catch: clips are capped at 10 seconds. If you want a 30-second Short, you stitch generations in Flow\u2019s scene builder.\n\nOmni Flash accepts any combination of text, images, audio, and video as input \u2014 and reasons across all of them simultaneously. You can hand it a reference image of a character, an audio file with dialogue you\u2019ve recorded, and a clip showing the lighting style you want, then have it generate one output that resolves all three constraints. ...                         "text": "Found some real tutorials. Here's the picture, then the videos:\n\n**Access**: Since you have a Google One/AI subscription, you get Gemini Omni Flash through the **Gemini app** and **Google Flow** (Google's video editor/scene-builder tool) \u2014 that's actually the better surface for your use case since Flow is built for multi-scene, longer projects.\n\n**For your 30-35 sec video**: clips cap at 10 seconds per generation, so the real skill is generating consistent 4-5 shots and stitching them in Flow's scene builder \u2014 that's exactly what a couple of these tutorials cover.",

                    {
                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-16T12:25:58.804926Z",
                        "stop_timestamp": "2026-08-16T12:26:05.540750Z",
                        "flags": null,
                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "A few practical tips pulled from what these guides emphasize, so you get the most out of them:\n\n- **Use one clean reference photo of yourself** \u2014 good lighting, front-facing, neutral background \u2014 and feed it in first before writing any scene prompts. This is what the model locks onto for consistency.\n- **Write your prompts in stages, not one giant paragraph**: start broad (\"me sitting at a desk talking to camera, office background\"), generate, then refine turn-by-turn (\"now have me smile,\" \"warmer lighting,\" \"add hand gesture\"). Omni is built for this conversational editing loop rather than one perfect prompt.\n- **Restate consistency details every time** you start a new segment/shot \u2014 same outfit, same lighting style, same setting \u2014 because separate generations don't automatically inherit context unless you're editing forward in the same thread.\n- **Stitch in Flow's scene builder**, not a separate video editor, since that's what keeps the character/style anchored across your 30-35 second timeline.\n- Every clip gets an invisible **SynthID watermark** for AI-provenance \u2014 expected and not removable, just so it doesn't surprise you.\n\nOne more thing worth knowing: for genuinely \"very realistic\" results, output is capped at 720p-1080p on Flash, so if you want the sharpest possible final render, generate in Omni and do a light upscale/color pass afterward \u2014 several of these guides mention that as standard practice rather than a workaround.\n\nWant me to help you actually draft the shot-by-shot prompts for your specific video once you know what it's about?",
                        "citations": []
                    }
                ],

                "uuid": "01a00aea-b840-7046-b324-f2cfc0886c43",
                "text": "Yes, I'm familiar with Google AI Studio \u2014 it's Google's web-based platform for developers to prototype and experiment with their Gemini family of models. Since Google's tools evolve fairly often, let me confirm the current details are accurate rather than relying purely on memory.\n```\nThis block is not supported on your current device yet.\n```\n\nYes \u2014 Google AI Studio is Google's web-based platform for building with the Gemini model family. My original picture of it (a simple prompt-testing playground) is pretty outdated, though \u2014 it's evolved a lot through 2026. Here's where it stands now:\n\n**Core idea:** It's a browser-based environment where you can prototype with Gemini models \u2014 text, image, audio, video \u2014 test prompts, and build apps, largely for free, with usage-based billing kicking in via the Gemini API once you scale.\n\n**What's new this year:**\n- Major updates in May included Gemini 3.5 Flash, managed agents via an \"antigravity\" harness, native Android app creation, and one-click export to Antigravity (Google's separate agent-first IDE)\n- Gmail, Drive, and Sheets connectors were added in June, letting you build apps that work directly with your Workspace data without leaving the platform\n- A \"Design Variations\" feature launched in late June that generates and applies new UI layouts with one click, plus a \"Design Mode\" for directly editing interface elements in the preview without touching code\n- A mobile app is in the works so you can iterate on code and preview builds from your phone\n- By late June, nearly 200,000 apps built in AI Studio had been deployed and shared for free in a single month\n\n**Worth knowing:** as of terms effective March 2026, Google has clarified that AI Studio/the free Gemini API tier isn't intended for consumer-facing products \u2014 so if you're building something people other than you will use, it's worth reading the current terms and considering paid billing.\n\nIs there something specific you're trying to do with it \u2014 build a prototype, compare it to another tool, understand the pricing?",

                                "text": "For millions of builders, Google AI Studio has become the fastest path from prompt to a production app. At Google I/O 2026, we reimagined not just how you build, but what you can build, too.                                 "text": "2026-05-13: Google DeepMind launched interactive experiments in Google AI Studio, including an AI-enabled mouse pointer concept aimed at exploring next-generation interface design. 2026-05-20: Google rolled out major updates across Google AI Studio and the Gemini API, including Gemini 3.5 Flash, ...\n\nFor AI Product Managers, Google AI Studio matters because it compresses the path from idea to interactive prototype. Instead of waiting for a full engineering cycle, PMs can validate UX concepts, compare model behaviors, explore multimodal use cases, and test integrations with Google ecosystem tools like Gmail, Drive, and Sheets. Its steady stream of launch updates also signals that Google sees AI Studio as a front door for both rapid experimentation and broader developer workflows. 2026-05-13: Google DeepMind launched interactive experiments in Google AI Studio, including an AI-enabled mouse pointer concept aimed at exploring next-generation interface design. \n\n2026-05-20: Google rolled out major updates across Google AI Studio and the Gemini API, including Gemini 3.5 Flash, managed agents with the antigravity harness, native Android app creation in AI Studio, workspace integrations, and one-click antigravity export.\n\n2026-06-02: Integrated Gmail, Drive, and Sheets connectors were added to Google AI Studio, enabling users to build and test apps without leaving the platform; tester management inside AI Studio was also introduced. 2026-06-14: Logan Kilpatrick described Google AI Studio\u2019s long-term goal as removing friction from building AI and eventually bringing that simplicity to businesses more broadly. 2026-06-27: Design variations launched in Google AI Studio, allowing builders to generate multiple alternative UI directions after creating an app; design previews during background builds were also highlighted. \n\n2026-06-28: Logan Kilpatrick reported that nearly 200,000 apps built in Google AI Studio were deployed and shared for free in the prior month, suggesting s",

                            {
                                "type": "knowledge",
                                "title": "Google AI Studio Update: What's New in June 2026 - SolidAITech",
                                "url": "https://www.solidaitech.com/2026/07/google-ai-studio-june-2026-update.html",
                                "metadata": {
                                    "type": "webpage_metadata",
                                    "site_domain": "solidaitech.com",
                                    "favicon_url": "https://www.google.com/s2/favicons?sz=64&domain=solidaitech.com",
                                    "site_name": "SolidAITech"
                                },
                                "is_missing": false,
                                "text": "Google AI Studio in June 2026 is more capable than it was at I/O \u2014 Gemini 3.5 Flash as the default model, the new Starter Tier, and Managed Agents all genuinely lower the effort required to go from idea to deployed prototype.\n\nWhy this matters beyond the legal fine print: if you're building a consumer-facing product on top of AI Studio or the free Gemini API tier, this language gives Google a clear basis to say you're outside the intended use case if something goes wrong. That's a materially different risk position than a year ago. Official Terms Effective March 2026 ai.google.dev/gemini-api/terms \u00b7 Beyond the terms language, three concrete product changes landed after I/O that are worth knowing about if your picture of AI Studio is a few months stale. \n\nGemini 3.5 Flash is now the default model \u2014 reached general availability on June 8, 2026, and Google positions it as delivering near-Pro intelligence at Flash-tier speed and cost \u00b7 Managed Agents API in public preview \u2014 a single API call now provisions a fully-equipped autonomous agent running in a secure, isolated Google-hosted sandbox \u00b7\n\nGoogle AI Studio in June 2026 is more capable than it was at I/O \u2014 Gemini 3.5 Flash as the default model, the new Starter Tier, and Managed Agents all genuinely lower the effort required to go from idea to deployed prototype. It's also more clearly scoped than it was. The \"not for consumer use\" language isn't a dramatic lockout, but it is Google formally closing the gap between \"free developer sandbox\" and \"free personal chatbot\" that a lot of casual users had been quietly exploiting. \n\nIf you're building something real, the direction is clear: enable billing, set a spend cap, and treat AI Studio as the professional tool its own terms now say it is. Not in the sense of technically blocking access \u2014 you can still open Google AI Studio and use it today with a standard Google account. What changed is the official terms of service, which now explicitly state that Google A",

                        "start_timestamp": "2026-08-16T14:12:34.778293Z",
                        "stop_timestamp": "2026-08-16T14:12:41.765799Z",
                        "flags": null,
                        "type": "text",
                        "text": "Yes \u2014 Google AI Studio is Google's web-based platform for building with the Gemini model family. My original picture of it (a simple prompt-testing playground) is pretty outdated, though \u2014 it's evolved a lot through 2026. Here's where it stands now:\n\n**Core idea:** It's a browser-based environment where you can prototype with Gemini models \u2014 text, image, audio, video \u2014 test prompts, and build apps, largely for free, with usage-based billing kicking in via the Gemini API once you scale.\n\n**What's new this year:**\n- Major updates in May included Gemini 3.5 Flash, managed agents via an \"antigravity\" harness, native Android app creation, and one-click export to Antigravity (Google's separate agent-first IDE)\n- Gmail, Drive, and Sheets connectors were added in June, letting you build apps that work directly with your Workspace data without leaving the platform\n- A \"Design Variations\" feature launched in late June that generates and applies new UI layouts with one click, plus a \"Design Mode\" for directly editing interface elements in the preview without touching code\n- A mobile app is in the works so you can iterate on code and preview builds from your phone\n- By late June, nearly 200,000 apps built in AI Studio had been deployed and shared for free in a single month\n\n**Worth knowing:** as of terms effective March 2026, Google has clarified that AI Studio/the free Gemini API tier isn't intended for consumer-facing products \u2014 so if you're building something people other than you will use, it's worth reading the current terms and considering paid billing.\n\nIs there something specific you're trying to do with it \u2014 build a prototype, compare it to another tool, understand the pricing?",

                "uuid": "01a00aec-1627-7cc8-952e-39d60ef39dec",
                "text": "Good, that narrows it down nicely. Now tell me about the app itself \u2014 what should it actually do, and what's the data you want it working with?\n\nSpecifically it'd help to know:\n\n1. **What the dashboard is for** \u2014 what problem does it solve, who's using it?\n2. **The data** \u2014 what's in it (rough structure/columns), where does it live (a spreadsheet you'll upload, Google Sheets via the connector, static file), and roughly how much of it?\n3. **Key views/features** \u2014 e.g. charts, filters, tables, search, editing capability?\n4. **Look and feel** \u2014 any style preference, or should I leave that open for AI Studio's Design Variations to explore?\n\nJust describe it in plain language \u2014 I'll turn it into a tight, well-structured prompt for AI Studio once I have the shape of it.",
                "content": [
